# Rules conformance

Where this engine's behaviour stands against the **official Riftbound Core
Rules**, and — just as importantly — which behaviours have never been checked.

**Reviewed through:** Core Rules `2026-07-16` · Core Rules Patch Notes
`2025-10-24` · Origins card errata (first batch, 31 cards)

## How to use this file

- Cite **rule numbers**, never pasted rules text. The Core Rules PDF lives in
  `docs/` but is git-ignored (Riot's content, ~39 MB).
- **Read it with `pdftotext -q -raw`, NEVER `-layout`.** This line used to say
  `-layout`, and that instruction is where the wrong citations in this file came
  from: `-layout` puts the rule numbers in a column that does not line up with
  their text, so a number read off one row belongs to another. Three were found
  in a single day, two of which were PDF line numbers being cited as rules.
  `-raw` emits the document in reading order with each number attached to its own
  text:

  ```bash
  pdftotext -q -raw "docs/Riftbound Core Rules Updated 2026-07-16.pdf" - | grep -n "the sentence you mean"
  ```

  Rows added before 2026-08-08 predate this and have not all been re-checked
  against `-raw`. See CLAUDE.md for the four citations already corrected.
- `Status` is the point of the whole document:
  - **Conformant** — checked against a rule, and we match. The rule number is
    the evidence.
  - **Divergent** — checked, and we don't match. Must say why (deliberate
    scope cut, or an open bug).
  - **Unverified** — the behaviour exists and works, but nobody has confirmed
    it against the rules. **This is the default and there is a lot of it.**
- **Never upgrade a row to Conformant without a rule number.** A file that
  implies conformance it hasn't earned is worse than no file — that is exactly
  how end-of-turn healing stayed wrong through 372 passing tests.

### On the Java oracle

`A:\Projects\riftbound-engine` remains the best *implementation* reference in
this project — resolver structure, ordering, edge cases someone already hit.
It is **not** a rules authority: it has been wrong at least twice (Master Yi's
aura, which its own audit comment admits, and the combat-cleanup healing scope
this engine inherited). A grep of our source found **93** "Mirrors <the Java
oracle>" claims against **5** citations of an actual rules section. Those 93
are `Unverified`, not `Conformant`.

## Verified

**Standing ruling from the project owner, 2026-08-14: this engine is a digital
version of the PAPER game, and "done" means every card implemented.** Two things
follow that this table should be read against.

**A legal play is never withheld.** If the rules let a player do it, the
enumerator offers it and the player decides whether it is sensible. This is NOT
the same as the engine's long-standing "if uncertain whether it is legal, do not
offer it" rule, which stands and which every offered-then-refused crash here
argues for. The distinction is uncertainty versus substituting our judgement for
the player's. UNL-140 Conscription's "pay 5 XP for a target you already had" was
withheld on the second grounds for about an hour and is now offered.

**Every Divergent row below is a debt, not a decision.** Nothing is out of scope
for being systemic — the battlefield-replacement mechanism, a fourth payment
bucket for a foreign-domain pip, and mid-resolution play under 419.3.b are all
in, however many files they touch.

| Rule | Behaviour | Rule # | Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| Damage healing timing | Heals at two moments only: end of each player's turn, and Combat Cleanup | 143.3 / 317.2 / 466.1 | `turn-manager.runEnd`, `combat.resolveShowdown` |
| "You may play me for [Cost]" replaces the BASE cost | A replacement, not a discount, so it may be DEARER than the print — UNL-025 Undying Legion's trash price is his printed 3 Energy **plus** a Fury pip. Implemented as a swapped base handed to the same pricing path the printed cost takes, at all THREE cost sites. `powerDomain: null` in a replacement is the `[rainbow]` pip and reuses `matchesPowerDomain`'s existing "any domain" reading | 356.1.a | `engine/replaced-costs.ts`; consumed in `legal-actions`, `validate-play-card`, `execute-play-card` |
| An APPLIED COST on a Game Action — the Standard Move's first price beyond its exhaust | UNL-163 Mageseeker Investigator, which **204.4 names as its own worked example**. 144.2 makes exhausting the unit the entire cost of a standard move, so before this the move path carried no payment at any site. 204.4.b ("paid as the Game Action is performed; they do not use the chain and cannot be reacted to") is why it belongs on the move path rather than in any effects registry, and 204.4.c ("if a player can't pay or chooses not to pay, they cannot perform the associated Game Action") is why an unpaid group move is REFUSED rather than merely untaxed. The barred thing is the ACTION and 144.3 makes a simultaneous multi-unit move one action, so moving the same units one at a time stays free. `MoveUnitAction.payment` carries rainbow runes only; the tax recycles them with no floating-Energy credit, the line 164.2 already draws for a `[Deflect]` surcharge | 204.4 / 204.4.b / 204.4.c / 144.2 / 144.3 | `engine/move-surcharge.ts`; `validate-move-unit`, `execute-move-unit` |
| A Power pip in a domain the CARD does not print | UNL-146 Syndra - Transcendent's "while I'm in a showdown, your spells have [Repeat] [2][Chaos]" — granted to spells of every domain, so the first Fury spell cast beside her owes a Fury pip and a Chaos pip on one play. `RunePayment` had three buckets and none can say that: `powerRunes` is checked against the CARD's `powerDomain` (demanding Fury for the Chaos pip, refusing a legal play) and `rainbowRunes` against no domain at all (paying a Chaos pip with a Body rune, stronger than printed). `foreignPowerRunes` is a fourth bucket with a third rule — a NAMED domain that is not the card's — re-derived at each site from the granted spec rather than carried on the action. **Her arrival is what makes `RepeatCostSpec.domain` live**: it was dead data, correct only because all fourteen printed Repeats are in their own card's domain. The pip's runes are RESERVED out of the pool before the general payment is computed, since Energy is domain-agnostic and would otherwise spend the only Chaos rune | 820.1.c.1 / 416 / 164.2 | `engine/repeat-grants.ts`; `legal-actions`, `validate-play-card`, `execute-play-card` |
| A STANDING `[Repeat]` grant, as opposed to an armed one | Temporal Portal ARMS `PlayerState.nextSpellRepeatGrants`, a counter spent by the next spell played. Syndra's is true of every spell for as long as she stands in a showdown, so riding that counter would have granted one repeatable spell per arming. A separate source (`standingRepeatGrantFor`) that both pricing gates fall back to, re-derived from the board each time because "while I'm in a showdown" stops being true the moment she leaves. She is the pool's first "while I'm in a showdown" condition | 820.1.c.2 | `engine/repeat-grants.ts` |
| Two GRANTED `[Repeat]` instances at once resolve as one | Syndra beside an armed Temporal Portal. 820.1.c.2 makes them separately payable, so the rules allow paying both for two extra executions; `PlayCardAction.grantedRepeatPaid` is a single boolean and `card-effect-resolution` runs one extra execution for it, so the armed source wins and the second instance is not offered. **DIVERGENT, deliberate, and an UNDER-offer** — narrower than printed, which is the direction this engine errs in, unlike the alternative of paying one price for two executions. **UPDATE 2026-08-14: the missing shape now EXISTS and this row was NOT closed by it.** `repeatExecutions` made the PRINTED instances a per-instance list for Curtain Call, and the handoff predicted that list would close this too. It does not, and the reason is worth keeping: a printed instance is identified by its INDEX into `repeatCostsOf(defId)`, and a granted one has no index — it is identified by its SOURCE (an armed `nextSpellRepeatGrants` counter, or Syndra standing in a showdown), which is re-derived from the board at every pricing site rather than enumerated. Closing this needs the granted sources to become an enumerable list first; the execution list is then the easy half | 820.1.c.2 | `legal-actions` granted-repeat branch |
| A restriction BETWEEN two announced targets | UNL-106 Repulse — "choose a friendly unit at a battlefield. Counter an enemy spell or ability that chooses IT and NO OTHER friendly unit." The pool's only such constraint, and the PDF uses this card by name as its worked example of announce-time selection. It cannot be a filter on either target: `counterFilter` answers about the spell alone and `eligibleTargets` about the unit alone, so `choosesOnlyThisFriendlyUnit` is a PAIR predicate applied where the pair first exists — the cross product in `legal-actions` — and re-derived in `validate-play-card`. **"No other friendly UNIT", not permanent**: a chosen friendly gear does not block the counter, and an enemy unit chosen alongside is irrelevant. The printed "at a battlefield" is load-bearing (355.9.b narrows where a bare noun would be widened by 355.9.a.1), so a friendly unit in base is not protectable. Deliberately NOT approximated as Not So Fast's `choosesFriendlyPermanent`, which is wider than printed in three directions at once | 355.7 / 355.8 / 355.9.b | `card-effects.ts` (`choosesOnlyThisUnit`, `enemyOnly`); `counter-spell.choosesOnlyThisFriendlyUnit`; `legal-actions`, `validate-play-card`; `effects/body.ts` UNL-106 |
| "As you play this, NAME A TAG" is asked when the Gear lands, not at announce | UNL-138 The List. 355's Make Relevant Choices puts "as you play this" at ANNOUNCE, so the strict home for the name is a field on `PlayCardAction` fanned out by the enumerator. **DIVERGENT, deliberate, and measured rather than preferred**: the pool has 111 distinct tags and `legal-actions` fans choices out unconditionally, so a 1-Energy gear would push 111 PlayCard actions before targets — and the AI evaluates every action it is offered, a cost measured the same day on the move fan-out (~120s to over ten minutes). A parked DECISION costs one action to answer and builds its options only when asked. The window this opens is empty: a Gear does not use the chain (it goes straight into `activeGear`), so nothing can respond between announce and the naming. The name lives on `GearInstance.namedTag`, per instance, because two Lists name two tags. The option set is EVERY tag in the pool, not the ones on the board — naming is a read on what the opponent will play, and filtering it to what is visible would make the card strictly weaker than printed | 355 / 355.9.a.1 / 143.2.b | `engine/named-tag.ts`; `execute-play-card` gear placement; `effects/chaos.ts` UNL-138 |
| Several units may move as ONE Game Action, and the enumerator offers every grouping | 144.3 — "players may perform multiple Units' standard move simultaneously. This is treated as one game action performed on multiple Units." `legal-actions` emits every non-empty SUBSET of the units that can reach a destination, so the AI can declare (and be taxed for) a group move. It emitted one action per unit until 2026-08-14, which made UNL-163's applied cost reachable by a human client and by nothing else. Eligibility is unchanged and still asked per unit — ready, not locked by Vex - Apathetic, and `[Ganking]` to leave a battlefield. **BOUNDED above 8 movers**: beyond that only the singletons and the all-in group are emitted, because 2^n is honest and unbounded while the AI evaluates every action it is handed. The bound is exported as `groupedMoveTruncated` and asserted from both sides, so it is a stated limit rather than a silent truncation. **This moved `walkout` from 191/107/32 to 191/115/29** — same walkouts, same control awarded, 8 more points and 3 fewer battlefields closing empty, because the AI now arrives in force instead of one unit per action | 144.3 | `legal-actions` (`movableTo`, `nonEmptySubsets`, `pricedMove`) |
| Cards banished WITH a specific permanent, countable separately from the banish zone | UNL-181 Jhin - Virtuoso's "if there are four spells banished with me". `PlayerState.banished` is one flat list and every other writer of it (Arcane Shift, Void Rush, Time Warp) would poison a count taken from it, so "with me" is an ATTACHMENT: `LegendInstance.banishedInstanceIds`, the same field `GearInstance` already carried for The Zero Drive. Ids rather than copies, so nothing counting the banish zone sees them twice. The `spellCast` event gained `spellInstanceId` in the same change — every listener on it before Jhin read only a PRICE (Lux's "costs 5 or more", Revna's "you spent 4 or more"), and he is the first to act ON the spell. His threshold reads `energySpent`, not `totalCost`, for the reason recorded on Revna's row | 186 / 383.3 | `model/card.ts` LegendInstance; `effects/fury.ts` UNL-181; `triggers.ts` + `execute-pass-focus` for `spellInstanceId` |
| A DELAYED triggered ability, created by a resolved effect and belonging to no card | UNL-169 Ashe - Focused's "when they hold, return it to their hand (**even if I'm no longer on the board**)". The engine's two earlier delayed effects (`killDamagedUnitsThisTurn`, `buffUnitsPlayedThisTurn`) are booleans read inline by their firing site, which is right for a modifier and wrong for an ABILITY — 383.3 puts a Triggered Ability on the Chain with a response window. `TriggerChainEntry.source === "delayed"` is a real chain item whose `listenerDefId` exists only to name it in the viewer: nothing is looked up, and resolution reads `PlayerState.banishedUntilHold`. It takes the non-`event` sources' 809.1.b rule to its limit — a source that has left play is not an edge case here, it is the printed behaviour. The armed ids live on the CARD's owner, so "when THEY hold, return it to THEIR hand" needs no memory of who banished what, and the field is deliberately NOT cleared at `runEnd`: "when they hold" names no turn | 383.3 / 809.1.b / 469.2 | `engine/delayed-triggers.ts`; `scoring.ts` (fire), `execute-pass-focus` (resolve), `effects/order.ts` UNL-169 |
| Two delayed abilities waiting on one moment resolve as ONE chain item | Two Ashes against one opponent arm two delayed abilities, and 383.3 would place two items with a response window between them. This engine places one that returns both. **The same divergence, and the same reasoning, as `TriggerChainEntry.times`** for Karthus - Eternal and the three "triggers an additional time" cards: two readings of one situation would be worse than one reading applied consistently, and nothing can be done between two returns of cards nobody can interact with | 383.3 | `delayed-triggers.holdDelayedReturnTrigger` — **DIVERGENT, deliberate** |
| An activation cost may be paid by EXHAUSTING another friendly unit, and the effect may name the payer | UNL-045 Forgotten Signpost — "Exhaust a unit you control, [Exhaust]: move a different unit you control to the location of the unit you exhausted". `ActivationCost.exhaustFriendlyUnit` rides the same `costPermanentInstanceId` `killFriendlyPermanent` already rides (one question: which permanent did the cost name), and `ActivatedAbilityEvent` now carries that field so the resolver can read it. Ready units only — 416.3, there is nothing to take from an exhausted one — and units only, since a gear is not "a unit you control". **The destination is the PAYER's location, not a chosen one**, which is what lets a base destination work with no `destinationIsBase` on `ActivateAbilityAction`: `forceMoveToDestination` already dispatches on base, exactly as SFD-050 Azir's swap relies on. "A different unit" and "not where it already stands" constrain the PAIR, and the cost axis is fanned out per mode before any target exists, so `costPayerPairingAllowed` is called at the enumerator's cross and re-derived by the validator | 416.3 / 355.9.a.1 / 383.3 | `activated-abilities.ts` (`exhaustableFriendlyUnits`, `costPayerPairingAllowed`); `effects/calm.ts` UNL-045; `legal-actions`, `validate-activate-ability`, `execute-activate-ability` |
| An activation cost may depend on the TARGET the activation chose | UNL-188 Hextech Gauntlets — "[Equip] [3][rainbow]. This ability's Energy cost is reduced by the Might of the unit you choose." The one such cost in the pool, and `activationCostOf(defId, modeId)` cannot answer it: the target is chosen after the price is asked. `activationCostFor(state, player, defId, modeId, target)` sits in front of it and returns the old answer unchanged for every other ability; all three cost sites go through it. The reduction uses `effectiveMight` (143.2), not the printed number, and floors at 0 — a 9-Might unit does not pay you back. **With no target it returns the BEST case on purpose**, because `canPayActivationCost` and the enumerator's affordability gate both run before any target exists and pricing the un-reduced cost there would withhold the Gauntlets whenever only the discount made them affordable; each candidate is then re-priced against its own target in the fan-out, so nothing is offered that the validator will refuse | 143.2 / 414 | `engine/activated-abilities.activationCostFor`; `legal-actions` (`pricedFor`), `validate-activate-ability`, `execute-activate-ability` |
| A replaced cost is a passive that only applies in the zone it names | The permission does not exist while the card is elsewhere, so `replacedCostFor` checks zone membership itself rather than leaving it to its three callers. 366.1 works UNL-025 by name as its own example; 366.2.a is why a from-HAND replacement (UNL-089 Jhin - Meticulous Killer) is an ordinary cost alteration needing no 419.3.a exception | 366.1 / 366.2.a | `engine/replaced-costs.replacedCostFor` |
| The permission workaround for 419.3.b only reaches the ACTIVE player | A replay granted to somebody else is not merely unwritten, it is UNUSABLE. `timing.mayPlayCardNow` opens with `playerIndex !== actingPlayerIndex(state)`, a Default-timed spell additionally needs the active player's own `chainOpen && turnState === "Neutral"`, and a grant cleared at `runEnd` never survives to the grantee's turn | **STILL TRUE OF THE PERMISSION PATH, and no longer a blocker for any card. CLOSED 2026-08-14 by not taking that path.** UNL-020 Dancing Grenade's "its controller may play this spell again" is a PARKED DECISION: `legal-actions` returns the pending decision's answers and nothing else while one is outstanding, and it returns them for `decision.playerIndex` whoever that is — so the grantee gets a real window instead of one that never opens. The pip is paid inside the resolution by `payPowerFromChanneled`, asked speculatively when the options are built so an unpayable offer is never made (416.3). **The refusal was right about its blocker and wrong about its fix, which is the third time that pattern held this week** — it also claimed "this engine cannot pay mid-resolution", which was stale: Flame Chompers, Mistfall and Here to Help all do. The `fromPlayerIndex` field built for this card and removed the same day was correctly removed; nothing needed it | 419.3 / 419.3.b / 416.3 | `effects/fury.ts` UNL-020's two decisions; `test/dancing-grenade.test.ts` |
| A `[Repeat]` cost is paid in the CARD's domain, and `RepeatCostSpec.domain` is unread | Both pricing sites fold the repeat's Power into `card.powerCost` and pay the total with `card.powerDomain`; nothing reads the spec's own `domain` field. Correct for all fourteen PRINTED Repeats, which are each in their own card's domain — `repeat-cost-table.test.ts` asserts that card by card, which is why the unused field has gone unnoticed. It breaks the moment a repeat's domain differs, which is UNL-146 Syndra - Transcendent granting `[2][Chaos]` to "your spells": a Fury spell would owe one Fury pip and one Chaos pip, and `RunePayment`'s three buckets (`energyRunes`, domain-checked `powerRunes`, any-domain `rainbowRunes`) have no room for the second. Recorded as a live limitation rather than a divergence, since no implemented card reaches it | 820.1.c.1 | `card-effects.RepeatCostSpec`; refusal at `effects/chaos.ts` UNL-146 |
| "When you draw your Nth card each turn" needs the ORDINAL on the event | UNL-074 Frigid Jewel. `cardDrawn` carries `nthThisTurn` rather than leaving a listener to re-read `PlayerState.cardsDrawnThisTurn`, because the trigger is HELD (383): by the time it resolves the count has moved on, so every held instance of a single "draw 3" would see the same final number and fire three times or none. Raised once per CARD from inside `drawCards` — the one funnel every draw goes through, the Draw Phase's included | 383 | `triggers.GameEvent` (`cardDrawn`), `effect-helpers.drawCards`, `effects/mind.ts` UNL-074; `test/frigid-jewel.test.ts` |
| In COMBAT, a damage replacement applies to the ASSIGNMENT, not to the damage | **465.2.c.5**, whose worked example quotes UNL-013 Lotus Trap verbatim: an attacker assigning to a doubled unit assigns half and "1 damage that doubles to 2 damage as it is assigned to the unit. When that damage is dealt, it doesn't get doubled again." So the doubling has two homes: `dealDamage` multiplies what it deals, while combat HALVES what the unit costs to kill (`assignmentNeeded`, rounded up) and `applyDamage` restores it. They cannot compound because combat never routes through `dealDamage`. The halving is observable only when a doubled unit frees pool for ANOTHER target — with one defender `distribute` caps at the pool and dumps the remainder, which is why the tests use two | 465.2.c.5 | `GameState.damageDoubledUnitInstanceIds`, `combat.assignmentNeeded`, `damage-modifiers.damageIsDoubledFor`; `test/lotus-trap.test.ts` |
| A Lethal Damage override needs no per-marker damage attribution | **142.4.c** names UNL-118 Elder Dragon by hand — "This alters the Lethal Damage value for enemy units that have damage marked by you." The qualifier looks like it demands attribution on `UnitInstance.damage` (one unattributed number), and the card was refused twice for that. Measured, both sites that ask already know: `dealDamage` is handed the `casterIndex`, and combat damage to one side comes from the other by construction, so `remainingMight` asking about the opposing seat IS the attribution. The override lives in the lethal TEST and in `remainingMight`, not in `modifiedDamageAmount` — the Dragon does not turn a 1 into a 5, he makes 1 enough | 142.4.a / 142.4.b / 142.4.c | `damage-modifiers.anyDamageIsLethalTo`; `test/elder-dragon-lethal.test.ts` |
| **DIVERGENCE** — a Lethal Damage override arriving AFTER the damage does nothing | 142.4.a makes Lethal Damage "the amount of marked Damage that will cause a unit to die IN A CLEANUP", so an Elder Dragon played while an enemy already carries damage his controller marked should kill it at the next cleanup. This engine kills at the moment damage is dealt and `runCleanup` has no lethal sweep, so there is nowhere for that to happen. A property of the damage model rather than of this card, and the only case that would need the per-marker attribution the refusal predicted | 142.4.a | `effect-helpers.dealDamage` (kills inline), `cleanup.runCleanup` (no sweep) |
| Competing Replacement Effects are ordered by the AFFECTED object's controller | **372** — "If more than one Replacement Effect applies to the same event being executed, then the controller of the object being acted on determines the order." So UNL-007 Smite's "banish it instead" is checked BELOW Highlander's death ward in `killUnit`: the object is the dying unit, and its controller would always take the ward that saves it over a banish that does not. That makes ward-first the controller's choice rather than a tie this engine broke. Smite's rider is armed for the TURN and applies to the spell's own kill as well as later ones, so it is armed BEFORE the damage; 808.1.d.1 makes the replaced death not a death, so no [Deathknell], no death-watch and no `unitsLostThisTurn` | 372 / 369.1 / 808.1.d.1 | `GameState.banishOnDeathUnitInstanceIds`, `effect-helpers.killUnit`, `effects/fury.ts` UNL-007; `test/smite-banish-replacement.test.ts` |

So UNL-117 Arachnoid Horror's "if an enemy unit is alone there" asks whether the OPPONENT has exactly one unit at that battlefield, and says nothing about how many the caster has. The obvious reading — the only unit present at all — is STRICTER than printed and would refuse legal plays on exactly the boards the card is for. Strictly narrower than Deadbloom Predator's `occupiedEnemyBattlefield` (`>= 1`), which is why it is its own grant kind rather than a reuse | 740.2.a / 740.1.a | `unit-triggers.enemyUnitIsAloneAt`; `test/arachnoid-horror.test.ts` |

The payout is `deploy.unitEntersReady` beside `acceleratePaid`, NOT an on-play trigger: 369.3 makes "I enter ready" a replacement describing how the unit enters, and the trigger route is observably different — the unit would sit exhausted through the response window and fire `unitReadied`. The condition needed a ninth spell-named `PlayerState` field; the eight existing ones are censused in `unl-chaos-wave8-refusals.test.ts` and none counts plays | 204.2 / 369.3 | `card-effects.OPTIONAL_POWER_COSTS` (`condition`), `PlayerState.spellsPlayedThisTurn`, `deploy.unitEntersReady`; `test/crescent-guardian.test.ts` |
| An optional additional cost may buy a PRICE, not just an effect | 204.2 makes "you may spend 3 XP as an additional cost" a real Additional Cost, and 356.1 puts what it buys — "if you do, I cost [3] less" — in the base-cost-modification step. UNL-178 Poppy is the pool's first where the payout is the price itself, so her paid variant is a second PAYMENT rather than a flag on the first, priced at all three cost sites and above the affordability bail (her discount is what makes her affordable at all with 4 runes). A per-UNIT predicate is asked at BOTH doors (`runAwaken`'s two inline maps and `readyUnit`) and at a third site that is easy to miss: the `awakened` capture that raises `unitReadied` events, since a unit that did not ready must not announce that it did. **This closed a LIVE divergence** — UNL-144 Maduli readied every Awaken and was STRONGER than printed, pinned in two test files that both flipped red when it landed | 315.1.b.1 / 415.1 / 415.3.b | `board-restrictions.unitMayBeReadied`; `test/maduli-cannot-be-readied.test.ts` |

Recorded on `PlayerState.replacedCostPlays` because nothing about the card sitting in the trash can re-derive that a specific kill happened; keyed by instanceId so a second copy in the same trash is granted nothing, and consumed on use because 419.3.b's window is one play. "If it HAD 3 [Might] or less" is measured with `effectiveMight` BEFORE the kill (143.2's current Might), not the printed number | 356.1.a / 419.3.b / 143.2 | `effects/signature-fury.ts` UNL-186; `replaced-costs.grantReplacedCostPlay` / `holdsGrantedReplacedCost` |
| Two trash-play permissions, only one of which is spent | Last Rites' `trashUnitPlaysThisTurn` grants a FULL-COST play of any unit and is consumed; a card's own printed "play me from your trash for [Cost]" grants a price with the zone and is not. Both can hold at once, so the action's `replacedCostPaid` decides which was used and the charge survives a replaced-cost play | 419.3.a / 356.1.a | `timing.mayPlayFromTrash` (the zone question) vs `timing.mayPlayFromTrashOnCharge` (the counter); split spent in `execute-play-card.usedTrashCharge` |
| Healing scope | Global — Combat Cleanup step 3c is literally "Heal all Units", not just combatants | 466 step 3c | `effect-helpers.healAllUnits` |

The unit's `scope: "anywhere"` is 355.9.b's bare-noun reading — “a friendly unit” carries no location word, so base counts |

A "your tokens enter ready" ability is a REPLACEMENT EFFECT (369.3, worked through with Master Yi, Honed: "the event of him entering exhausted is replaced by one where he enters ready"), and 375's second example ignores a generating-effect modification that "cannot apply". So Renata Glasc - Industrialist **overrides the printed "exhausted"**, and 185.2.d's "tokens have a type" makes an unqualified "your tokens" reach GEAR tokens too | 149.1 / 184.1 / 185.2.d / 369.3 / 375 | `board-restrictions.tokensEnterReady`, applied at `token.placeToken` and `token.placeGearToken` |

| A Ready rune pays BOTH 1 Energy and 1 Power | Exhaust it for Energy, then recycle the (now exhausted) rune for Power — `payPowerFromChanneled` credits 1 floating Energy when it recycles a READY rune, and `computeAutoPayment` lets a Ready rune spent on Power still count toward Energy | 164.2 | **Conformant, and now CITED.** *"A Basic Rune always has the following two Abilities: `[E]: [Reaction] — Add [1].`"* and *"`Recycle this: [Reaction] — Add [C].` The Power added this way corresponds to the Domain of the Rune that is being Recycled."* They are two SEPARATE abilities: exhausting costs the rune's readiness, recycling costs the rune itself, and a Ready rune can do both in sequence. An already-Exhausted rune can only produce Power. This underpins three things that were previously justified only by the Java oracle's comment: `payPowerFromChanneled`'s floating credit, `computeAutoPayment`'s double-duty, and the finding that the survey's "activation Energy+Power double-spend" is self-cancelling rather than a bug — the cancellation is the rules' own, not an accident. **First observable on OGN-242 Baited Hook**, whose `[1 Energy][Order Power]` cost is payable off a single Ready Order rune |

Out of combat it is unchanged. **Non-circularity no longer comes from `isCombat: false`** — combat Might reads `effectiveKeywords`, which reads Fiora - Victorious's "while I'm [Mighty] … [Shield]", which reads `isMighty`. **476 settles that without a tie-break**: "each effect … is applied … only a single time across all sequences", so her `[Shield]` is granted in Ability-Altering and re-checked only AFTER Arithmetic already produced 5, and cannot be part of the arithmetic that made her Mighty. `MightContext.mightyCheck` implements exactly that by withholding `dependsOnMight` grants and nothing else — a `[Shield]` from Taric, a battlefield or the card frame still counts. Removing that flag produces a stack overflow, which is how it is proved to be doing work, since the combat-only terms are exactly the `[Shield]` she is granted BY being Mighty |

## Divergent

**Audited as a whole on 2026-08-07** — 60 rows, for the first time. What the sweep
did and what it found, so the next one can start from here rather than repeat it:

- **Every code identifier this table names still exists.** Mechanical check:
  every backticked identifier in the 60 rows, grepped against
  `packages/engine/src`. One miss, and it was `svellsongur.test.ts`, which lives
  in `test/`. So the table has not drifted by RENAME — it drifts by outcome.
- **Three rows were stale, all in the same direction: something recorded as
  BLOCKED had since shipped.** "Controller vs owner" (Hostile Takeover, closed by
  one optional field), "Legend triggered abilities on the Chain" (said four hooks
  were blocked; all but `onBeginningPhase` are held, and one blocker was never
  real), and this table's "TWELVE event kinds" (21 of 22 now). All three are
  corrected in place with the correction dated, not deleted.
- **A stale note had already been found and fixed in the CODE and left here.**
  `legend-abilities.ts`'s `onUnitPlayed` clause says in as many words that "the
  recorded blocker for this hook was wrong" — and the row recording it, plus that
  file's own header comment, both still said it was blocked. **If you unblock
  something, grep for the note that said it was blocked.**
- **Claims of absence were re-read against the code and HOLD**: Prevent (437) is
  still unmodelled, `GearInstance` still has no `keywords` field, `activeGear` is
  still player-level, `destinationBattlefieldId` still carries a battlefield id
  and nothing else, and `DeathknellEffect` is still a bare resolver with no
  `applies`/`capture` while `DeathWatchDefinition` has one.
- **The trigger census is now a TEST, and it WAS wrong.** `test/trigger-census.test.ts`
  recomputes it from the registries' own accessors: **231 held / 3 inline of 234
  trigger cards**, against the "110 / 3 of 113" this table carried. The old figure
  predated SFD finishing and missed all 96 of its trigger cards. Writing the test
  also turned up a key in the trigger registries that is **not a card** —
  `SFD-184-conquer-home`, a granted ability — which a naive union counts as one.
  Two mutations prove it can fail, one of them the exact "count `mightBonus` as a
  trigger" miscount this row's own text warns about.
- **Still with no instrument, and still undated**: "39 cards" ([Deflect]), "23 of
  the 72 gear" (Ornn), "17 cards" (Pending vs Finalized). Same shape, not yet
  measured — treat them as of their writing date, not as current.

| Rule | Should be | We do | Rule # | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A player walking their own unit home | **A MOVE.** 455 defines a Recall as a relocation to base "**without it being a Move**", and 446.1 makes any permanent changing position from one space on the Board to another a Move — with 107.1.b making a Base a Location. The rules' Recalls are SYSTEM relocations: 457.1's automatic gear recall and 446.1's "corrective Recall" | **A Move — corrected 2026-08-09.** It exhausts (144.2) and is gated by `mayMoveToBaseFrom`, and it now holds `unitMoved` (`to: "base"`) and `unitMovedFrom`, and increments `movesThisTurn` | **455 / 446.1 / 107.1.b**, with 456.1 ("Recalls do not cause Triggered Abilities to trigger that are triggered by Move actions") being the sentence that must NOT be applied here | **WAS DIVERGENT, NOW CONFORMANT — and recorded because the misreading was mine and it was load-bearing in three places.** Reported from playtesting: "I moved a treasure hunter back from a BF and it did not generate a gold gear token." I first called that correct, citing 456.1 — a true sentence about Recalls, applied to something that is not one. The engine's action is named `RecallUnit` after the Java oracle, and the NAME is what made the wrong rule look applicable; two tests had encoded the same reasoning with the same citations. The action is still called `RecallUnit` (renaming reaches the web, the AI and every fixture); the name is wrong and the behaviour is now right, which is the safer order. **It also exposed a card bug**: Mister Root reads "when I move TO A BATTLEFIELD" and his `applies` never checked the destination — harmless only while no walk-home event existed, and he began paying 2 XP for going home the moment one did |
| Where a played unit TOKEN lands | **185.2.a**: tokens are played "following all the applicable steps for playing a card plus any restrictions or modifications from the effect that created the token", and the inherent restriction on playing a Unit is that "it can only be played to the controlling player's base or a battlefield they control". So a token whose creating effect names no destination gets the ordinary CHOICE | **Base, silently**, unless the defId is in `card-effects.TOKEN_PLACEMENT_SPELL_DEF_IDS` — an allowlist of three | **185.2.a**, with 350.2 ("Tokens are not cards, but can still be Played") and the inherent-restriction sentence quoted under 806.3/813.3 | **DIVERGENT, and it reaches a HARD-GATED SET.** Found 2026-08-09 by a wave-3 agent working on UNL-044, and the evidence is an inconsistency the engine already contains: **Sprite Call (OGN-094) and Desert's Call (SFD-031) print the IDENTICAL shape** — "Play a [N] Might [X] unit token", no destination clause — and Sprite Call is in the allowlist while Desert's Call is not. Only Recruit the Vanguard (OGS-015) actually prints the parenthetical, so the allowlist is not tracking what the cards say. One of the two must be wrong, and 185.2.a says it is Desert's Call. **The resolver already reads `destinationBattlefieldId`, so the fix is one table row per card** — the enumeration is the only missing half. Also reaches UNL-044 Flurry of Feathers, whose four Birds always land in base. Not fixed here because it changes behaviour in a declared-complete set and deserves its own change with the fan-out re-measured |
| "If I didn't conquer this turn", asked of a WEARER | The question is about the unit wearing the Equipment — did THAT unit conquer | **Answered positionally, per PLAYER.** The engine records `conqueredBattlefieldsThisTurn` on the player, so a wearer that walked in AFTER the conquest reads as having conquered, and one that conquered and then moved away reads as not having | — (no rule assigns a conquest to a unit; 383.4.c only makes a unit "participating in… present during the Conquer action") | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-09 with UNL-019 Blighted Battleaxe**, the first card to ask it. Narrow: it needs the wearer to change location in the same turn as a conquest at that battlefield. Per-unit conquest attribution does not exist and would be a real addition, so this is filed rather than fixed |
| An `endOfTurn` ability lands after the board has healed | The instruction resolves at the end of the turn it triggered in | **Held, so it resolves in the NEXT player's Action phase** — after `runEnd` has healed the board | — (the standing turn-boundary divergence this file already records for held end-of-turn triggers) | **INHERITED, not new — recorded 2026-08-09 so UNL-019 is not diagnosed twice.** Blighted Battleaxe deals 4 to its wearer at end of turn, and that 4 lands on a wearer whose marked damage has just been cleared. It changes what the card kills. Same root as every other held `endOfTurn` trigger; listed here because the effect is DAMAGE, where the healing makes the difference visible rather than academic |
| An effect-driven relocation is a MOVE | **446.1**: "A Permanent changing its position from any space on the Board to another space on the Board is a Move", and **449**: "Spells, Abilities, or other effects may cause a Move to occur." | **Fires `unitMoved` and `unitMovedFrom` — CLOSED 2026-08-09.** Both force-move helpers in `effect-helpers.ts` emit through one shared function, so the two cannot disagree about what a move is. | **446.1 / 449** (446.2 is the contrast: "a card changing game zones does not in itself constitute a Move", which is why a bounce-to-hand correctly fires nothing) | **WAS DIVERGENT, NOW CONFORMANT.** Seventeen implemented cards watch a move and every card-driven relocation was silent to all of them. The pin in `test/unl-chaos-wave2.test.ts` is flipped to assert the right answer rather than deleted. **The two smaller gaps this row used to name are BOTH closed as of 2026-08-09, and the sentence is rewritten rather than appended to, because a stale "still open" here reads as work outstanding and gets re-done.** `movesThisTurn` is incremented — the fix was to count it at PLACEMENT rather than at event time, on the `arrived` object the helpers already build, which sidesteps the re-finding problem the old note described. And `causedByIndex` has a consumer: UNL-133 Blast Cone's second clause is written, gating on `event.causedByIndex !== listener.ownerIndex`, which is the "when YOU move an ENEMY unit" shape the field was added for |
| A `[Level N]` triggered ability's gate, when checked | **727.1.c.1**: "Triggered Abilities of Dependent Keywords must be Active for their trigger to be evaluated" — so the XP is read when the trigger FIRES | **Read at RESOLUTION.** On-play unit triggers are held Chain Pending Items (383), and `UnitTriggerDefinition` has no `applies` hook — `EventTriggerDefinition`, `DeathWatchDefinition` and `DeathknellDefinition` all do | **727.1.c.1**, with 727.1.c.1.a ("if a Triggered Ability becomes active at the same time as its trigger condition would be fulfilled, it triggers") and **824.1.d** for the other direction — the ability goes Inactive the moment XP drops below N | **DIVERGENT — found 2026-08-09 by a card agent, reachable in BOTH directions.** XP crossing the threshold during the response window wrongly ENABLES the trigger; XP spent during it wrongly DISABLES one that should have fired. Live on **UNL-040 Wuju Apprentice**. The missing field is one `applies` hook on `UnitTriggerDefinition` beside the `holdUnitTrigger` call in the shared `engine/unit-triggers.ts` — which is why no domain-file agent could close it |
| XP spent as an ACTIVATION cost | **204.1.b**: "On activated abilities, the Base Cost is the resource or instruction written before the ':'." So "Spend 2 XP:" is a base cost, paid at the cost step | **Spent inside `resolve`**, gated by `availableWhile: canSpendXp(...)`. `ActivationCost` has no `xp` field | **204.1.b**, and 203.3 for the refusal ("if the game action associated with a Cost is impossible … they cannot pay the Cost and they will not execute the linked Effect") | **DIVERGENT but currently UNOBSERVABLE, and recorded so it does not become observable unnoticed.** This engine resolves an activated ability INLINE with no chain item, so there is no window between paying and resolving in which the difference could be seen — the same reason `counter-spell.ts` already records for abilities. It becomes real the day activations go on the Chain. Three cards ride it: **UNL-102**, **UNL-126**, **UNL-162**, plus **UNL-158**'s `[Equip] — Spend 1 XP`, which 818.1.c.3 permits ("Equip costs may include both resource costs and NON-RESOURCE costs"). The gate is enforced by BOTH the enumerator and the validator via `canPayActivationCost`, so it is a real refusal rather than a resolver shrug. One `ActivationCost.xp` field closes it |
| `[Vision]` on a GEAR | **817.1.a**: "It is present on Permanents" — a Gear is a Permanent, so a Gear may carry `[Vision]` | **The Unit path cannot reach it.** `applyVision` is private to `unit-triggers.ts` and called only from `dispatchOnPlayUnit`; `legal-actions.ts` and `validate-play-card.ts` both gate `visionRecycle` on `card.kind === "Unit"` | **817.1.a**, with 402.1 putting a triggered "you may" at finalization | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-09.** Reachable on **UNL-161 Divining Shells**, whose `[Vision]` half was implemented as a per-card decision instead. That implementation is arguably MORE conformant than the Unit path (402.1 wants the choice asked, and it is), but it deliberately does not fire `holdCardsRecycled` or offer `offerTopOfDeckBanish` — because `applyVision` does neither, and a Gear behaving differently from a Unit would be a worse divergence than the one being fixed. Both gaps belong to the Unit path, not to the card |
| A spell played from a NON-HAND zone, when a player chose it | **355.8**: "All targets are declared during finalization" — a spell someone chose to play should be aimable | **Aimable when the chooser is present — CLOSED 2026-08-11 for the single-unit shape.** `playCardIgnoringCost` takes an optional `choices`; Fizz - Trickster parks a second question and passes the answer. A multi-slot or destination-carrying spell still resolves against nothing | **355.8** for the declaration, **359.3.e.11** ("do as much as you can") for what an unaimed spell still does | **WAS DIVERGENT AND REACHED EVERY TARGETED SPELL FIZZ PLAYED — found by playtesting, not by any instrument**, reported as "spells played with fizz dont seem to do anything". `playSpellImmediately` resolved with an EMPTY choice set on the argument that "the choices a spell needs are made when it is ANNOUNCED, and nothing announced this one". That is right for a card played with no player in the loop (a top-of-deck flip, a Deathknell) and wrong for Fizz, who ASKS which spell — the chooser is already answering, so a second question costs nothing. Hextech Ray left the trash, dealt 3 to nobody and was recycled: the card did its whole job and the spell did none of its own. **The remaining limit is deliberate**: only `{kind:"unit"}` is asked, because offering half of a multi-slot spell's choices is worse than offering none — the player answers and the rest still fizzles silently |
| "Your X effects trigger an ADDITIONAL TIME" | **383.3** places one chain item per trigger, so an ability that triggers twice should produce TWO Pending Items — with a response window between them | **ONE chain item, executed twice.** `TriggerChainEntry.times` / `HeldDeathknell.times`, looped at resolution | **383.3**, with **820.1.d** as the contrast: the rules use the phrase "an additional time" only for `[Repeat]`, and there it explicitly means "execute the instructions of this chain item one additional time during resolution" — one item | **DIVERGENT, DELIBERATE, and applied CONSISTENTLY to all three cards that print it** — OGN-236 Karthus - Eternal, UNL-029 Red Brambleback, UNL-087 Blue Sentinel. Karthus was implemented this way long before the other two; when they landed on 2026-08-11 the choice was to match him or out-correct him, and matching won: two readings of one printed sentence inside one pool is worse than one reading applied everywhere, and changing a shipped OGN card's chain behaviour deserves its own change rather than a rider. **What is actually lost is narrow**: the opponent cannot respond between the two executions. Nothing else differs — the doubling is counted at the moment of the event either way (383), so killing the doubler in the window does not undo it. **Closing it means changing all three together**, which `test/trigger-doublers.test.ts` enforces: it asserts that the chain still holds ONE entry per listener, and that all three cards still print the same sentence, so moving one forces the others |
| A `[Level N]` clause that adds a TARGET | **355.8**: targets are declared at finalization, so a clause that is Inactive should not be offering one | **The slot is offered at any XP and the RESOLVER gates it.** Below the level a caster may name the extra target and it does nothing | **355.8**, with **824.1.b.1** ("while you have N or more XP") and **824.1.d** (Inactive below it) | **DIVERGENT, and an over-OFFER rather than an over-reach — recorded 2026-08-11 with UNL-038 Skyward Strike** ("Move an enemy unit. `[Level 6][>]` `[Stun]` an enemy unit"). A `TargetingSpec` is STATIC — it is read per card, not per board — so it cannot ask whether the controller has 6 XP. The effect is never wider than printed; only the offer is, which is the direction this engine errs in everywhere. Pinned by a test asserting the second slot IS offered at 0 XP, so a spec that ever learns to ask the board fails loudly instead of quietly narrowing |
| Two same-role targets on one card, when the card says neither "another" nor "different" | Nothing forbids naming the same unit twice, so "move an enemy unit, stun an enemy unit" should permit moving and then stunning the SAME one | **The two slots must be DISTINCT units** — `legal-actions` enumerates slot-0 × slot-1 excluding the pair where both are the same | — (no rule requires distinctness; the restriction is the enumerator's) | **DIVERGENT and NARROWING — recorded 2026-08-11 with UNL-038 Skyward Strike.** Reaches every `unitSlots` card whose two slots share a role and whose text omits "another", and it removes a real line: moving a unit out of a fight and then stunning that same unit is something the card's wording allows. Not fixed with the card because the distinctness is enforced in the shared enumerator and changing it moves every slot card at once — Facebreaker and Zenith Blade among them, where the two targets genuinely must differ |
| A unit LEAVING the board keeps its damage and counters | **124.1**: "Whenever a Game Object changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone, all Temporary Modifications of all kinds cease to be tracked… Damage is cleared. Counters are removed." | **The two zone exits DISAGREE.** `returnUnitToHand` clears damage and modifications; `effect-helpers.completeDeath` files the instance into the trash having stripped only the Buff (705) | **124.1**, with 705 for the Buff that IS stripped | **DIVERGENT and ENGINE-WIDE — recorded 2026-08-10 by a wave-6 agent working on UNL-142.** Reachable by every play-from-trash card in the pool (Soulgorger, The Harrowing, Last Rites, Fizz): a unit killed with 4 damage on it returns from the trash still carrying the damage. The agent found it by proximity rather than by any instrument, and pinned it with a labelled wrong-answer test. Not fixed inside a card wave because it changes what four shipped cards return |
| An alternate PRINTING of an implemented card | The same card, printed again — every UNL Legend has a plain, `(Overnumbered)` and `(Signature)` print, and five Poros are reprints from earlier sets | **Aliased to the canonical print — CLOSED 2026-08-10.** `printingAliases()` derives the map from the base name; `mergeRegistries` expands it, and the sites comparing a defId to a literal go through `canonicalDefId` | — (a data/identity question, not a rules one; **185.1.a** is the nearest, making a token's nature permanent by contrast) | **WAS SILENTLY BROKEN, NOW CONFORMANT.** All 31 printings are distinct ids, which is correct — a deck list names a printing. But every registry here is keyed by defId, so **12 printings with an implemented twin had no implementation of their own**: a deck holding the Signature print of Rengar - Pridestalker had a Legend with NO ability. Coverage reported them "unimplemented", which reads as *not written yet* rather than *silently broken*, so nothing could see it. The derivation is checked rather than trusted — `printing-aliases.test.ts` asserts every alias matches its twin on rules text (reminders stripped), type, cost and Might, so a print that genuinely diverged fails loudly. **Two follow-on bugs were caught by that same test file**: the alias made `effective-might` apply an aura three times (it is the one consumer that iterates registry VALUES), and `PARTIALLY_IMPLEMENTED` was not canonicalised, so an alternate print of a half-written card reported WHOLE |
| A conditional "don't deal combat damage" aura over ENEMY units | A unit whose text silences other units' combat damage by a live Might comparison — "enemy units here with less Might than me don't deal combat damage" | **They deal full damage.** `combat.ts`'s `outgoingMight` consults `DEALS_NO_COMBAT_DAMAGE_DEF_IDS`, a flat set keyed by the defId of the unit BEING silenced | **143.2** for "current Might" (the comparison reads live on both sides, per the UNL-105 row below), with 466 step 2 for the damage step | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-060 Vilemaw**, the first card in the pool to silence someone ELSE. The existing table fits SFD-082 Ezreal - Dashing, who silences himself, and cannot express an aura at all. **Two rejected fixes are the useful part of this row**, both argued down by the agent that hit it rather than assumed: expressing it as a `mightModifiers` outgoing-Might floor RECURSES, because the enemy's own Might is the input to the comparison that would modify it; and it would also strip Mightiness through `isMighty`'s outgoing branch, silently changing what the victim is rather than what it deals. The real fix is a predicate seam in `outgoingMight` taking `(state, victim, battlefieldId)`. Pinned by a test in `test/unl-calm-wave5.test.ts` — and that pin asserts a **death**, not a damage count, because 466 step 3c heals every unit at end of combat and the first version of the assertion passed only by accident of expecting 0 |
| `[Backline]`, as a printed keyword | **465.2.c**: "I must be assigned combat damage last" — the mirror of `[Tank]`'s "first", with everyone else between | **Honoured — CLOSED 2026-08-10.** `combat.assignmentOrder` reads the keyword, and additionally a `[Backline]` GRANTED by an effect | **465.2.c**, with 465.2.c.7 (same-priority units may be assigned in any order) | **WAS DIVERGENT for four cards, NOW CONFORMANT — and the gap was never the mechanism.** The three-tier sort had been in `combat.ts` since Caitlyn - Patrolling was written; what it consulted was `ASSIGNED_LAST_DEF_IDS`, a one-entry allowlist, because when it was written `"Backline"` was not in `model/keyword.ts`. The keyword was added when Unleashed landed, and from that day the parser populated something nothing asked about while the coverage flag blamed four cards on a mechanism that already existed. **One `hasKeyword` call**; UNL-043, UNL-141 and UNL-145 came whole. Caitlyn keeps the allowlist because she prints the sentence as prose and carries no bracket at all |
| A unit that is BOTH `[Tank]` and `[Backline]` | **465.2.c.8**: the requirements conflict, so the player assigning damage "may choose to assign damage to Caitlyn first, fulfilling the Tank requirement, or last, fulfilling the Backline requirement" — but never in the middle | **Resolved in Tank's favour, always.** The player is not asked | **465.2.c.8**, whose worked example is Caitlyn - Patrolling given `[Tank]` | **DIVERGENT, and deliberately so — restated 2026-08-10 now that the keyword makes it reachable on five cards rather than one.** Tank-first is one of the two legal answers, so the outcome is never illegal; what is missing is the CHOICE, and this engine has no interactive damage assignment to ask through. Reachable today because Block grants `[Tank]`. **Worth reading with the note at `assignmentOrder`**: the guard that implements the tie-break is measured-redundant for observable outcomes — dropping it survived mutation, because the unit then appears twice in the order and `distribute`'s arithmetic produces identical survivors and identical excess damage. So the divergence is in what the player is asked, not in what the board does |
| A per-unit MOVEMENT LOCK ("they can't move it this turn") | An effect forbidding one specific unit's controller from moving it for the rest of the turn | **Unenforced.** `validate-move-unit.ts` gates on exhaustion, `movesThisTurn` and destination legality; nothing carries a per-unit, per-turn prohibition | **423** governs the `[Stun]` that accompanies it and IS honoured; the lock itself is an ordinary play restriction with no keyword | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-150 Vex - Apathetic**, the first card to print one. **Much narrower than the text suggests, which is why it is filed rather than built**: a unit that was just played arrives exhausted and an exhausted unit already cannot move, so the two clauses coincide except when something readies the victim — `unitsEnterReadyThisTurn`, `[Accelerate]`, or an "I enter ready" clause. Pinned by a test in `test/unl-chaos-wave5.test.ts` that asserts the wrong answer, and that pin was proved to discriminate: with the arrival-exhausted guard removed the legal-move count is genuinely 0, so `toBeGreaterThan(0)` is measuring the lock and not the exhaustion |
| "Double my Might", on a unit in COMBAT | The doubling reads the Might the unit actually has in that combat, including its combat-role terms | **Doubles the NON-combat effective Might.** `[Assault]` and `[Shield]` are modelled as combat-role terms behind `ctx.isCombat`, so a Vi attacking with a granted `[Assault]`, or defending with `[Shield]`, doubles the smaller figure | **432.1**'s worked example is this instruction almost verbatim ("Shield 2 … in combat as a Defender … current Might is 5, so it gets +5"), with **807.1.c** (`[Assault]` is "While I am an attacker, I have +X [M]") and **814.1.c** for `[Shield]` | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-030 Vi - Hotheaded.** Deliberately not fixed for one card, because **Last Stand (OGN-069) — the pool's other doubler, in a hard-gated set — reads it the same way**, so fixing Vi alone would make two cards with one instruction disagree. The faithful call is `{isCombat: true, isAttackingSide, combatRole: "remaining"}` derived from `attackerIndexAt`. Pinned by a test asserting the wrong number, and that pin was mutation-proved live: making Vi combat-aware kills it |
| An ACTIVATED ability whose cost scales with the board | "This ability costs `[1]` less for each friendly unit with `[Temporary]`" — priced when the ability is offered | **Always the printed `[4]`.** `ActivationCost.energy` is a plain number and `activationCostOf(defId, modeId)` is handed no state at all | **205** (what a cost is) with **414.4** for activation | **DIVERGENT — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-189 Lillia - Bashful Bloom**, the first activated ability in the pool whose price depends on the board. **The direction is the safe one and that is why it was left**: it under-offers and never over-offers, so nothing crashes. The reason it is not a small fix is the shape of the seam — FOUR sites price an activation (`canPayActivationCost`, `payActivationCost`, the enumerator's payment, the validator's re-derivation), all through the stateless `activationCostOf`, and a discount that reaches some but not all of them is precisely the offered-then-refused split that has produced four probe-caught crashes in this engine. Pinned by a test in `test/unl-signature-wave5.test.ts` |
| The MIGHT of a token that "becomes a copy" | Does a Reflection token copying a 5-Might unit have 5 Might, or the 0 its own definition gives it? | **0 Might.** The copy reproduces Name, Super Type, Type, Tags, Cost, Domain and Rules Text; Might is not touched | **477.1.b.1.a** for the seven copyable traits, **187.6** for the token ("A **0 [M]** Reflection token is a domainless unit token with 0 Might" — the Might is part of its name), and **477.1.a.1** for the contrast | **CONFORMANT — settled 2026-08-10, and settled by the SHAPE of the rules rather than by one sentence, which is why it is worth a row.** A wave-5 agent implemented it at 0 and flagged it as needing a ruling, on the grounds that a 0-Might Mirror Image (3 Energy + 2 Power) looks like a design absurdity. It is not: **477.1.a** lists the inherent traits and then adds a DEDICATED `477.1.a.1` saying "Assignment of Might is dealt with in this layer", while the copy sub-rule's seven-item list has no equivalent companion. The authors wrote a Might clause exactly where they wanted Might and declined to write one for copies, so the omission is structural, not an oversight. What a Reflection buys is the rules text and tags, and a 0-Might body still holds a battlefield and still counts for presence. **Decides the whole cycle**: UNL-081 Keeper of Masks (live), UNL-199 LeBlanc - Deceiver and UNL-200 Mirror Image (both still unwritten, both needing a copy subsystem whose subject is an arbitrary board unit) |
| "Another unit you control **at a different location**" | 355 makes an unmet targeting restriction an invalid choice, so a same-location pair should not be offerable | **Offered, validated and cast.** The swap then silently no-ops and the "Draw 1" still happens | **355**; `TargetingSpec.unitSlots` has `sameBattlefield` and no inverse | **DIVERGENT and STRONGER than printed — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-083 Smoke and Mirrors.** The bad direction: the card becomes a 2-Energy unconditional cantrip whenever the caster picks two units standing together. The fix is small and fully located — a `differentLocation?: true` member on `unitSlots` in `card-effects.ts`, one `continue` beside the `sameBattlefield` line in `legal-actions.ts`, one check beside the same in `validate-play-card.ts` — and was deliberately not taken by the agent because all three files are shared and five agents were writing at once. **Pinned by a test asserting the wrong answer.** A second, subtler face of the same gap: played from `[Hidden]`, `legal-actions` confines BOTH slots to the hidden battlefield, so every offered pair is same-location and the from-Hidden mode does nothing but draw — and **811.1.d.2.a works this exact card by name**, ruling that a second choice whose restriction makes the confinement impossible "can be chosen from any location" |
| Two units swapping places, "each to the other's location" | Simultaneous | **Sequential**, so the first arrival can apply Contested before the second unit has left | 355 | **DIVERGENT, narrow — recorded 2026-08-10 with UNL-083.** No simultaneous-move primitive exists; every relocation in this engine goes one unit at a time through the two force-move helpers. Observable only through Contested status and any trigger that reads occupancy between the two legs |
| A unit or gear TOKEN entering the board | **185.2.a**: a token "can be played by their owner if their card type is played, following all the applicable steps for playing a card", and **350.2**: "Tokens are not cards, but can still be Played" | **Both halves honoured — CLOSED 2026-08-10.** `placeToken` and `placeGearToken` hold a `cardPlayed` carrying `isToken: true`; "play a UNIT"/"a gear" listeners see it, "play a CARD" listeners are gated against it | **185** ("Tokens are not cards"), **185.1.a** (the nature is permanent), **185.2.a**, **350.2**, with **185.3.a.1** for the token having no printed cost | **WAS DIVERGENT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AT ONCE, NOW CONFORMANT.** Neither token path held any event, so nothing fired — which silently narrowed every unit-reading listener (Cithria of Cloudfield was never buffed by a Recruit; Pit Crew never readied for a Gold token) while making the card-reading ones accidentally right. **The two sentences are genuinely different and the fix had to honour both**, which is why this is one row and not two: a token IS played and a token is NOT a card. **Viktor - Innovator is the reason this needed a gate rather than just an event** — he makes a Recruit token when you play a card, so the first build that emitted the event LOOPED, and two existing tests failed with "the chain never reopened". Darius - Trifarian needed the same gate for a subtler reason: his "second CARD in a turn" reads `cardsPlayedThisTurn`, which a token never increments, so an ungated trigger would have asked `=== 2` at a moment the counter had not moved. **Nothing in the type system can catch a missing gate** — a listener that forgets `isToken` does not go silent, it wrongly fires — so every card-reading listener is named in `test/token-played-event.test.ts`, which is the only thing watching. Found from two directions in one hour by two agents: as why UNL-150 Vex never fired on a token, and as why UNL-058 Lillia could not be written at all |
| A unit TOKEN entering the board, as a "played card" | **185.2.a** makes a created token played "following all the applicable steps for playing a card", so a token unit arriving should be visible to "when a unit is played" listeners | **Invisible.** `token.placeToken` holds no `cardPlayed` event — it places the instance directly | **185.2.a**, with 350.2 ("Tokens are not cards, but can still be Played") | **DIVERGENT and ENGINE-WIDE — recorded 2026-08-10, found from two directions in one wave.** The Chaos agent hit it as the reason UNL-150 Vex never fires on a token; the Calm agent hit the same silence as the reason UNL-058 Lillia - Protector of Dreams ("when you play a token unit") could not be written at all. Every `cardPlayed` listener in the pool inherits it. The fix is one held event in `engine/token.ts`, but `GameEvent` has no token-entry kind and the widening would change what several shipped cards see, so it is filed rather than taken inside a card wave |
| A triggered ability whose "you may" is the FIRST part of its effect | The controller decides **at finalization**, and declining "is removed from the chain and considered to have not triggered" (383.3.a.2) — so a declined trigger never sits on the Chain for anyone to respond to | **Asked at RESOLUTION**, via `parkDecision`, for every "you may" on a triggered ability. The item goes on the Chain, Focus passes, the opponent may respond, and only then is the controller asked whether they wanted it at all | **383.3.a / 383.3.a.2**, and 402.1 / 402.1.a for the same step of the finalization procedure. **383.3.a.3 is the contrast that makes this a real split**: a "you may" in any LATER part of an effect *is* decided on resolution (the rules work Ornn, Blacksmith as the example), so the current behaviour is correct for one family and wrong for the other, and the engine does not distinguish them | **DIVERGENT — found 2026-08-08 while fixing the seven decisions whose affirmative answer had no visible label.** Reachable on at least five cards, all of which read "you may" first: SFD-184 Relentless Pursuit's granted "When I conquer, you may move me to my base", SFD-091 Buhru Captain, OGN-152 Mistfall, OGN-006 Flame Chompers and SFD-169 Altar of Memories. Three of those pay a COST inside the "you may", which 383.3.b puts at finalization too ("the cost must be paid in order to finalize"). **NOT reachable on OGN-153 Overt Operation or SFD-154 Guards!** — both are Spell effects, not triggered abilities, so 383.3 does not reach them and asking on resolution is right. **PINNED** by `test/relentless-pursuit.test.ts` — "asks at RESOLUTION, not at finalization", which counts the Focus passes between the trigger firing and the question arriving and asserts the WRONG answer (> 0; the rules make it 0). Closing this should flip that test, not delete it. The Tideturner row below shows the mechanism a fix would extend rather than invent: `TargetingSpec.optionalChoice` already solved this shape by making the decline an ENUMERATED VARIANT instead of a parked decision |
| "Becomes [Mighty]" when an AURA arrives | A unit crossing 5 Might because a continuous aura appeared (a Garen - Commander walking in) has become [Mighty] and should fire Fiora - Grand Duelist | **Narrowed 2026-08-08 to: an aura SOURCE arriving, a unit MOVING into or out of a positional aura, and **ENTERING COMBAT** as a `[Shield]` defender or `[Assault]` attacker (measured 2026-08-08: `isMighty` false before staging and true after, chain empty).** Equipment attaches and positional auras are now seen | 709 / Fiora (SFD-205) | **DIVERGENT, and inherent to how Might is modelled.** `effectiveMight` is recomputed on every read from printed Might + buffs + this-turn modifiers + continuous auras + Equipment, so there is no stored total whose write could be the moment. `withMightTransitions` brackets the operations that change an INPUT and compares before with after; an aura arriving changes no input on the crossing unit, so no bracket contains it. Closing it needs the layer snapshotting recorded elsewhere as missing (rule 477) — the same gap that makes an arithmetic effect's application time unobservable. The reachable half is implemented and tested, including the three ways a level check would be mistaken for a transition: already-Mighty, not-reaching-5, and a DEBUFF through the same helper. **AMENDED 2026-08-08 after a playtest report, and the old wording was hiding two LIVE bugs rather than describing a clean limit.** `withMightTransitions` called `effectiveMight` with **no `battlefieldId`**, so every unit on the board was compared as if it stood in base and positional auras were uncounted entirely; and `attachEquipment` was not bracketed at all, though an Equipment's `+N` badge is part of current Might (Blade of the Ruined King is +4, and that is the set's main route to 5). Both fixed, bracketed at the single writer so all five attach sources get it. `granted-keywords.isMighty` had the identical missing-location defect — it also affected Fiora - Victorious's "while I'm Mighty", Volibear, and three "count your Mighty units" cards — and is fixed with them. A comment in `legend-abilities.ts` claiming "a 4-Might unit under a Garen aura counts as Mighty" was FALSE until this change and is corrected in place. What remains open is the two cases named above, both pinned by failing-if-closed tests; the move case is deliberately unfixed because a move reaches the board from four places and bracketing one would fire for a hand-moved unit and not a Charm-moved one. `Stand United`'s `extraMightPerBuffThisTurn` is a third, single-card instance |
| Ornn's rainbow Power paying a gear ABILITY's cost | "[Add] [rainbow]. The play half is fully implemented and is the bulk of the card: **23 of the 72 gear in the pool carry a Power cost**, measured. Closing the ability half means teaching `payPowerFromChanneled` to draw on floating pools, which changes every activation cost in the game and deserves its own pass | **UPDATE 2026-08-07, from a playtest report against Draven - Vanquisher** ("I want to pay manually as I may have Seals I want to use instead of recycling"): a Seal's output is ordinary `floatingPower`, and `payPowerFromChanneled` — the single funnel all 49 Power-payment sites go through — now spends floating BEFORE recycling a rune. That is a fix rather than a preference: a CARD's cost already spent floating first, so an ability ignoring it was an asymmetry between two paths for one resource; and floating EXPIRES at `runEnd` while a recycled rune returns, so floating-first is strictly better for the payer on every board. No choice is offered because a prompt would have exactly one right answer, forever. The tally for Sivir counts the FULL cost rather than only the runes recycled. **What remains is genuinely the restricted pools alone**: `restrictedSpellPower`/`restrictedGearPower` carry a card-KIND gate ("Spells only", "gear or gear abilities") and the funnel is not told the kind, so spending them there would be wider than the cards print. Pinned by `floating-pays-ability-costs.test.ts`, which asserts they are untouched.

| Enumerating a `[Repeat]`'s second choice set | 820.1.d lets the additional execution name different targets, so the full candidate list is the cross product of the two choice sets | **One variant, repeating the SAME choices**; the validator accepts any legal second set | 820.1.d | **Not a defect — the standing answer to an unbounded choice space**, and the same split `unitList` targeting and the repeatable additional costs already make. Two narrow readings are recorded rather than hidden: the Pit is APPENDED so the two presented battlefields keep their positions and every id in flight keeps its meaning; and it enters controlled by nobody, which is the state a battlefield starts a game in. (2) **The swap-back is AUTOMATIC where the card says "can be"** — *Unverified*. | `[Deathknell]` reading the board it died on | Noted as the Deathknell is added to the chain — **before completing the Kill Instruction** (808), and at Cleanup **3a for ALL lethally-damaged units before 3b trashes any** | **HALF CLOSED 2026-08-07.** `DeathknellDefinition.capture` now takes the note as the unit dies. The fix needs a primitive that does not exist — `DeathknellEffect` is a bare resolver with no `applies`/`capture` pair, unlike `EventTriggerDefinition` and `DeathWatchDefinition` which both have one. The agent explicitly refused the cheap workaround (reading the other deaths still on the chain), because placement is LIFO and a Poro killed FIRST would see none of them — right by accident about half the time. **UPDATE 2026-08-07 — the primitive exists now, and reading the PDF split this row in two.** `DeathknellDefinition` has the `applies`/`capture` pair, so this is the third family in line with `EventTriggerDefinition` and `DeathWatchDefinition` rather than anything new; `holdDeathknell` takes the note while the board it died on still exists and it rides the chain entry to resolution. **What that fixed is the KILL INSTRUCTION path**, and 808 is the rule: *"…it first has any such ability added to the chain as a Pending Item. All four sub-claims were verified in code rather than assumed: the ordering is structural (the additional term is floored and added after `modifiedEnergyCost`, so a whole-card clamp can never claw the pip back); `UnitCostSpec.mandatory` really does distinguish mandatory costs, and separately no mandatory cost in the pool carries a pip at all; Hard Bargain's ransom calls `payEnergyFromPool` and consults no cost modifier; and **`modifiedRepeatEnergy` reads `card.energyCost`, the definition's PRINTED field**, so a base-cost reduction cannot shrink a Repeat cost twice — confirmed on Find Your Center under a Temporal Portal grant, where the saving is exactly 2. Measured on the ruling's own example (Temporal Portal + Desert's Call): paying both instances costs 3 with Ezreal against 5 without, a saving of 2, and mutating the code back to sum-then-discount-once reports 4. Pinned by `test/ezreal-prodigy.test.ts` (19 tests). **The second half of this row stands**: the discount shares `targetDiscountAxis` with SFD-141 Irelia - Graceful, so with both live the player picks ONE axis serving both, which remains an unverified simplification |

If you do…" **"is not a cost of the ability, base or otherwise, but a game action being performed by a player"**. Contrast 820, which calls `[Repeat]` an Optional Additional Cost keyword, and 805.2 for `[Accelerate]` — those two ARE discounted. Pinned by two tests, so "it did not get cheaper" can never again be indistinguishable from "the discount never ran" |
| A GROUP move naming one destination, where a chosen unit is already there | 355.4.a excludes a unit's CURRENT location as a move destination — so is a set containing such a unit an illegal CHOICE, or a partial no-op? | **Partial no-op**: the choice is legal, the unit already there simply does not move, and the rest do | 355.4.a | **Project owner's ruling, and explicitly flagged by them as INFERENCE rather than a quoted ruling** — they searched the official rules docs, the RiftJudge Q&A database, riftboundfaq.com and the errata/FAQ pages and found nothing addressing it word-for-word, and declined to invent a citation. The reasoning: 355.4.a is phrased as a PER-UNIT check governing whether a given unit may move to a given destination, not as a rule about whether the location is a usable target; and Riftbound's general pattern for multi-part effects is that illegality in one sub-part does not cancel the whole effect. **Not yet implemented** — UNL-054 Tricksy Tentacles is refused for an unrelated reason (`unitList` targeting can express the group constraint but not the destination, see docs/unl-missing-primitives.md), so this is recorded ahead of the card. Worth confirming in the RiftJudge/rules Discord before it is built |
| Entering COMBAT can make a unit [Mighty], and nothing sees it | Under the higher-of-two ruling, a 4-Might `[Shield 1]` defender is Mighty the instant a Combat Showdown stages over it, so `unitBecameMighty` should fire | **No transition fires.** `isMighty` is false before staging and true after; the chain is empty | 476 / 709 / owner ruling 2026-08-08 | **DIVERGENT, measured, and deliberately not patched.** `withMightTransitions` compares a unit before and after an operation that changed one of its INPUTS; combat entry changes the CONTEXT and writes nothing about the unit, so no bracket contains it. It is also not one moment but four — Showdown staging, 316.8.b.1.a promotion of a NonCombat Showdown, a unit arriving at an already-open combat, and the combat closing — and bracketing the easy one would fire for some combats and not others, which is worse than firing for none. Same family as the two aura routes above. Pinned in `test/becomes-mighty-routes.test.ts` with the premise asserted BOTH ways so the pin cannot rot into a tautology |
| UNL-105's "an enemy unit here with LESS MIGHT THAN ME" | Measured when the trigger FIRED, or when the player answers? | **At answer time**, both sides of the comparison read live | **355.9.b / 355.8** | **CONFORMANT — settled by the project owner 2026-08-08 with the rule chain, and the existing behaviour was already right.** 355.9.b distinguishes a descriptive restriction baked into the TARGET ("kill a unit with 3 might or less" — restricts who may be chosen) from a condition bolted onto the effect ("kill a unit. If it had 3 might or less…" — evaluated later). "An enemy unit here with less Might than me" is the first form, so it restricts the choice; and **355.8: "All targets are declared during finalization"**, which for a held trigger is the moment the player answers, not the moment it fired. So anything in the response window counts. **Consequence worth knowing in play:** a unit buffed PAST his Might during the window stops being a legal choice, and if none qualify the trigger declines |
| A REPEATABLE optional additional cost carrying a pip | Is "spend any number of buffs" one cost paid N times, or N costs, for Ezreal - Prodigy's per-cost discount? | **One cost, one discount** | Owner ruling 2026-08-08, extending the Ezreal ruling above | **Recorded ahead of need — UNREACHABLE today.** The two repeatable optional additional costs in the pool (Kraken Hunter, Commander Ledros — "spend ANY NUMBER of buffs") are paid with permanents and carry no Energy or Power pip for the discount to reduce, so nothing observes this. It becomes live the moment a set prints a repeatable optional additional cost WITH a pip, and the answer is banked so that day is not a design discussion. Consistent with the parent ruling's "never twice to the same cost" |
| "HERE" in a triggered ability, after the source has moved | Is "here" the battlefield the trigger FIRED at, or where the source stands when the instruction executes? | **Re-checked on execution.** Moved away, or dead, and the instruction is moot | **359.3.f.1 / 359.3.f.2 / 359.3.f.2.a** | **CONFORMANT for five cards, settled by the owner 2026-08-08 — and the rule the codebase had been citing answers a different question.** 359.3.f.1 makes "here", "my" and "its" SOURCE referents; 359.3.f.2 says they are "checked on execution of the instruction", and its worked example is **Yasuo - Remorseful attacking, bounced home by Fight or Flight in the response window, so that "here" is no longer the battlefield and the attack trigger MISTARGETS**; 359.3.f.2.a makes an illegal referent return null with its instructions ignored. The counter-comments cited **383**, which fixes THAT an ability triggered — a different question. The genuine contrast is **359.3.f.3** (Lillia, Fae Fawn): information referenced from the TRIGGER CONDITION is fixed when the condition is met. Most are FREE (a doubled keyword is redundant under 817.1.a; an on-play trigger cannot re-fire, because the attach happens after the play) and the ability tables are done — `triggerCandidates` hands the copied key the listener `wearerListener` would have given it, so the chain gets a second entry naming the WEARER and resolution needed no change at all.
