| Optional on-play choice (“you may”) that TARGETS | Declining is a legal option, so an empty variant must be enumerated | **CLOSED 2026-08-07.** `TargetingSpec.optionalChoice` makes the enumerator push the no-target variant even when targets exist | 355 | **DIVERGENT, and it is an OGN card in a DECLARED-COMPLETE set.** Found 2026-08-05 by an SFD card-wave agent that declined to write a card on the claim. `effects/chaos.ts`'s own comment on OGN-199 Tideturner asserted the opposite (“enumeration offers the no-target variant too, so declining is a real option”) — corrected in place, and one more false note in a repo that has now recorded several. The decline appears exactly when there is nothing to decline. The RESOLVER already handles an absent target, so only the enumeration is wrong. **Swept: Tideturner is the ONLY card in the pool this reaches** — every other optional on-play choice is a parked decision, which can be declined. Not fixed: the `length === 0` rule is deliberate (a Unit is playable with no legal target, a Spell is not), so it needs a per-card “this trigger is optional” marker, and it changes behaviour in a complete set. **CLOSED 2026-08-07, and the marker is exactly that.** `TargetingSpec.optionalChoice` is read by the enumerator AND by `validate-play-card`'s `targetOmissionAllowed` — one flag, two readers, which is the only thing that stops a decline being offered and then refused, and each half was proved by a mutation that fails independently. It is deliberately NOT folded into the `length === 0` rule beside it: that one says something different ("a trigger with nothing to CHOOSE does nothing") and must keep applying to every mandatory on-play trigger, so folding them would quietly make all of them optional. The rule is **402.1** — "if the first part of a Triggered Ability's effect is 'you may', its controller decides whether or not to perform the Triggered Ability NOW" — at the Make Relevant Choices step, which is why the decline is an enumerated variant rather than a branch in the resolver, where it would already have been paid for. **`test/tideturner-optional.test.ts` also CENSUSES the flag**: it asserts Tideturner is still the only card carrying it, so a second one is a decision rather than something absorbed |

Cleared at `runEnd` with the rest of the turn. **This is also what made three already-written cards pay out**: `PLAY_FROM_ELSEWHERE_DISCOUNT_DEF_IDS` (Void Drone SFD-010, Drag Under SFD-164) and Rek'Sai - Breacher's (SFD-029) `[Accelerate]` grant are all keyed to `playedFromHand: false`, and were reachable only through the Champion Zone until the trash became a fourth play source. Asserted at BOARD level in `sfd-play-source.test.ts` — on the price the enumerator actually offers, not on a hand-passed `false`, which would have tested the rule instead of the wiring. **UNL-186 Death from Below joined this divergence on 2026-08-13** with the same shape and the same reason, differing in two ways worth stating: its permission names ONE INSTANCE rather than granting a per-player charge, and it carries a REPLACED price (356.1.a) rather than the printed one. SFD-202 shipped on 2026-08-07 and is whole: `borrowUnitInPlace` leaves the unit where it stands and applies Contested for its new controller (the half `takeControlOfUnit` could not do, since it recalls to the taker's base), and **one optional field, `UnitInstance.returnControlAtEndOfTurnToIndex`, is the entire memory the model lacked** — discharged by `runEnd`, cleared as the unit is handed back, and carried on the UNIT so a stolen unit that dies takes the obligation to the trash with it. That is the shape every re-read note in this repo has turned out to have: **the "missing subsystem" was one field.** What is genuinely unmodelled is still the general case — a permanent theft and a temporary one are told apart only by that flag's presence, so nothing can ask "who owns this" outside end-of-turn, and 190's owner/controller split has no representation. Row "Hostile Takeover's end-of-turn reversal" above covers the reversal's timing and already described the card as working, so this table also contradicted ITSELF for a day |

Nothing re-measured it when the set landed. The test recomputes from the registries' own accessors and asserts the shapes in use — the event kinds and the Legend hook keys — so a new one fails there and forces a decision instead of being absorbed into a count. **It also found that the registries hold a key that is not a card**: `SFD-184-conquer-home`, Relentless Pursuit's GRANTED ability, registered beside the real defIds because `holdEventTrigger` matches `grantedTriggersThisTurn` alongside `card.defId` — so a naive union over-counts cards by the number of granted abilities, and only comparing the registries against the card registry catches it. Two things it corrects, and the first correction was itself wrong the first time: **the "7 legend hooks" were never 7 cards, but they are not 9 either — there are 8 Legend TRIGGER cards.** 7 was a count of dispatch FUNCTIONS; 9 is the size of `LEGEND_ABILITIES`, which includes Master Yi - Wuju Bladesman, whose only entry is `mightBonus` — a continuous modifier recomputed inside effective-might.ts and never dispatched at all. Counting a table's keys and counting its dispatch shapes are two different wrong answers to "how many cards". The three rules that REMOVE a triggered ability from the chain are a replaced death (808.1.d.1), the controller declining to perform it, and declining to pay its cost — a dead listener is none of them. `TriggerChainEntry.listenerCard` carries the listener so resolution always has one, preferring the live board copy while it is still there. **`[Vision]` fires at the same MOMENT as an on-play trigger and is deliberately still inline** — one family at a time is what keeps a termination regression bisectable — so a Vision recycle now happens a chain-pop before the trigger of the same card. Measured on the Sett buff deck, 100 pinned games: max chain depth **6**, 2658 states with a trigger on the chain, 0 stranded, 100/100 terminated. `probes/chain-depth.ts` carries a scenario per converted family (see the rows below). The one part of 808.1.d.1 that IS honoured throughout: a replaced death never fires |

(1) **Every listener for such an event must take whose turn it was from the EVENT**, never from `state.activePlayerIndex`, which has already rotated — Sona - Harmonious would otherwise ready the opponent's runes, and would do it silently, since their pool is full from their own Awaken and nothing would look wrong. (2) **LIFO makes the ordering come apart**: the new turn's `unitReadied` and `battlefieldHeld` triggers are pushed after the old turn's `endOfTurn` ones, so they resolve FIRST. Fixing the moment properly means making the turn advance a multi-step state machine that can stop between End and Start-of-Turn for a response window, which is its own piece of work |
| Pending vs. Finalized chain items | Two distinct states. A trigger arrives **Pending**; its controller then completes the steps of playing it (choosing targets) to make it **Finalized**; priority is granted only once no Pending Items remain (345) | `pendingTriggers` models the Pending phase, but the Cleanup's finalize pushes entries that are already Finalized — **none of rule 402's four steps happens in between**. Measured 2026-08-04: **17 cards ask their question at RESOLUTION** where 402 puts it at finalization (OGN-006, 035, 037, 056, 063, 072, 107, 112, 152, 155, 162, 177, 228, 235, 249, 252, 261), and a further seven auto-select a target rather than choosing one at all | 337-345 | **"No trigger in this pool chooses a target" is stale** — Karma - Channeler is "when you recycle one or more cards, buff a friendly unit", a HELD trigger that names a unit, and Stealthy Pursuer is a second. Both work, by parking a DECISION when the trigger RESOLVES rather than choosing as it finalizes. So the divergence is observable now and has a definite shape: the choice happens one step later than the rules put it, which matters only if something could respond in between — and nothing can, because a pending decision suspends everything. Named here because `TriggerChainEntry`'s doc calls its contents Pending Items and the rules use that word more narrowly. **402 spells the missing step out in four numbered parts, and the engine has only the first.** 1. add the Pending Item (this exists). 2. *make relevant choices* — "if the first part of a Triggered Ability's effect is 'you may', its controller decides whether or not to perform the Triggered Ability NOW", then "make all choices required for this ability, such as targets, modes, or other relevant decisions", and "if there are not enough options to make legal choices ... remove it from the Chain now". 3. determine total cost. 4. pay costs, where "players may decline to pay for Triggered Abilities that have incurred a cost" and the ability is removed if they do. Steps 2–4 are all absent, which is why a "you may" is asked a chain-pop late, a cost is paid at resolution, and a trigger with no legal choice resolves to nothing instead of never becoming a Finalized Item. **The blocker is structural and is NOT about the triggers.** Finalization happens inside `cleanup.finalizePendingTriggers`, and a Cleanup cannot stop to ask anything: `withCleanupAndWinnerCheck` returns from `submit` BEFORE running the Cleanup whenever the decision queue is non-empty, which is 321 ("while Chain Items are Resolving, a Cleanup cannot occur") doing exactly its job. Asking at finalize therefore requires the Cleanup itself to become a resumable state machine that can suspend mid-step and continue on the answer — the same shape, and the same size, as the turn-advance state machine the turn-boundary row already names. Until that exists, moving any single card's question earlier would mean parking a decision from inside a Cleanup that is documented as unable to hold one. **The 2026-08-03 Attack Trigger conversion widened this considerably** — Crackshot Corsair, Leona - Determined, Twisted Fate, Yasuo - Remorseful, Ahri - Inquisitive and Teemo - Strategist all pick "an enemy unit here" and all pick it at RESOLUTION, and the PDF's worked example for Yasuo is precisely a trigger that targets as it finalizes and then MISTARGETS when the board moves under it. The auto-selection row below is the same gap seen from the card's side. **`EventTriggerDefinition.capture` is the nearest thing this engine has to the missing step**, and it is deliberately not a substitute: it notes a fact the ability triggered ABOUT (Mask of Foresight's "the unit that was alone"), which is not the same as choosing a target the opponent may then respond to |

| AI lookahead vs. responses | — | `heuristic-ai.settleDeferredResolution` settles each candidate assuming the opponent **passes** rather than responding | — | Not a rules divergence but a direct consequence of the timing work: that assumption was exactly true while nothing could be cast into a Showdown or onto a chain, and is now optimistic. **The 2-ply fix is BUILT and MEASURED, and it is switched OFF** — `EvalWeights.twoPly` exists and `settleDeferredResolution` honours it, but `BASELINE_WEIGHTS.twoPly` is `false`, because 2-ply ALONE measured 46.6% against the baseline (a loss) and only won when paired with a `cardInHand` weight (52.0%). The row is not "unfixed", it is "fixed, and the fix lost on its own" |
| Death replacement vs. the rest of a resolution | A replacement effect is settled before the resolution continues | Sett - The Boss's offer is parked as a pending decision from inside `killUnit`; the resolution that raised it (a combat exchange, a board wipe) runs to completion first, and only then is the question answered | 808.1.d.1 | **Measured, not theorised.** Ordering still diverges. **The STRANDING it used to cause is fixed (2026-08-01)** — see the row below; it was a separate bug, not this one |
| Winning mid-resolution | A player wins "**in a cleanup**" (194.2), and 321 forbids a Cleanup while a resolution is suspended — so points crossing the Victory Score while a question is outstanding is not yet a win | Conformant since 2026-08-01 | 194.2 / 321 | **Was a real, measured stranding bug and is worth keeping recorded.** `submit` gated on `winner(state)`, a bare points predicate, while `withCleanupAndWinnerCheck` correctly SKIPPED the winner check when a decision was pending. So an action that parked a question and then scored returned `Ok` with no win declared, and the next `submit` refused **every** action including the `AnswerDecision` that would have finished the resolution — leaving the unit in `unitsAwaitingDeathReplacement` forever, in neither play nor a trash. **5 stranded units per 300 self-play games; now 0, with all 5 won-while-pending answers accepted.** Two lessons: the two guards disagreed about whether the game was over, and the probe that found it had the SAME bug — it looped on `while (winner(state) === null)`, so it exited before submitting the answer and reported an unchanged 5 whether or not the engine was fixed. It had to be rewritten to stop on the declared `result`, the way the UI does, before it could measure anything |
| Battlefield abilities: the "when you hold here" seven | Each of the 24 printed Battlefields has rules text, and seven of them trigger on 469.2's Hold | **Implemented 2026-08-04.** `engine/battlefield-abilities.ts` is a sixth trigger registry keyed by `BattlefieldState.defId`, held as Chain Pending Items under a new `TriggerChainEntry.source: "battlefield"` and finalized by the same Cleanup as every other trigger. Altar to Unity, Grove of the God-Willow, Hallowed Tomb, Navori Fighting Pit, Reckoner's Arena, Startipped Peak and The Grand Plaza | 383 / 469.2 | **Battlefield abilities were never broken — they did not exist**, and the reason was structural rather than an omission: `card-loader`'s `shouldSkip` excludes Battlefield-type cards from `loadCardDefinitions`, so no `CardDefinition` existed for any registry to key off, and `holdEventTrigger`'s `Listener.card` is a `CardInstance` a battlefield is not. Four readings taken, all **Unverified**. (1) **A battlefield's ability is placed LAST at its moment, so it RESOLVES FIRST** under LIFO (340.1) — the same choice, and the same reason, as the Legend's position in `listeningPermanents`: the battlefield's printed effect is the frame the moment happens inside. 383 in fact lets a player choose the order among their own simultaneous triggers, and this engine fixes it. (2) **Reckoner's Arena's "activate the conquer effects of units here" runs those effects INSIDE the Arena's own resolution** rather than placing a Pending Item per unit — the Arena is one triggered ability and this is what it does, the same way a spell that kills three units is one chain item. It deliberately ignores each unit's `applies`: nothing triggered, so there is no trigger condition to test. (3) **Navori Fighting Pit's "buff a unit here" offers the HOLDER's units**, which is not a live distinction — a held battlefield has no enemy units on it by `isHeldBy`'s own definition — but does stop the ability reaching a unit that arrives between the hold and the resolution. (4) **The Grand Plaza counts its 7 units at RESOLUTION**, so an opponent can answer the seventh; counting at the hold would be a stronger card than the one printed. `PlayerState.chosenChampionDefId` is new and REQUIRED, for Hallowed Tomb: `championZone` is `null` exactly when the Tomb asks, and searching the trash for `isChampion` would offer the wrong card, since OGN prints 56 champions against 16 legends |
| Battlefield abilities: the "when you conquer here" five | Five of the 24 printed Battlefields trigger on 469.1's Conquer | **Implemented 2026-08-04**, held by the same `recordConquest` call that holds the permanents' and the Legend's conquer triggers, and placed after them so it resolves first. Monastery of Hirana, Sigil of the Storm, Targon's Peak, The Candlelit Sanctum, Zaun Warrens | 383 / 469.1 | Three readings, all **Unverified**. (1) **Sigil of the Storm's "you must recycle one of your runes" asks nothing**, because its own reminder text says so — "(This doesn't choose anything.)" — so the rune is taken in pool order. Which rune goes still decides which DOMAINS remain, the same simplification `payEnergyFromPool` already records. (2) **Targon's Peak arms a DELAYED effect on a per-player counter** (`readyRunesAtEndOfTurn`), fired by `runEnd` as an `endOfTurn`-moment battlefield trigger. It arms TWICE for two conquests in one turn: 471.1.b withholds the second POINT for a battlefield taken twice, not the second trigger. The count is CAPTURED at fire time and not re-read, because `runEnd` clears every "this turn" field before the trigger it fired resolves — the recorded turn-boundary divergence — so a resolution reading the counter would always find 0 and the card would silently do nothing. (3) **The Candlelit Sanctum's "put those you don't back in any order" IS asked**, but only when two cards survive: one card has no order. The recycles are asked one at a time rather than as a multi-select, for the reason Divine Judgment's cut records — an answer naming a set would have to be carried on the decision and validated against itself. **A battlefield's registry entry is a LIST of abilities**, and Targon's Peak is why: a card can print two abilities at two different moments, and a single-definition table could only have expressed that by resolving the delayed half somewhere nothing would report as part of the card |
| Battlefield abilities: the "when you defend here" two | Fortified Position and Reaver's Row trigger when their controller's side gains the Defender designation (464.2.c Step 1) | **Implemented 2026-08-04** from `cleanup.beginCombatAt`, the same instant every Attack Trigger fires, and fired ONCE per combat rather than per designation | 464.2.c / 383.4.e | Three readings, all **Unverified**. (1) **"You defend here" is a claim about the PLAYER, so it fires once as the combat opens and never from `designateArrivals`** — a player already defending does not begin to defend again because a reinforcement walked in. That is 383.4.e's "for the first time during a combat" applied to the side rather than to the unit, and it is the opposite of how the eight Attack Triggers read the same rule, because those are printed on units. (2) **Fortified Position's "choose a unit" offers BOTH sides' units at that battlefield.** The card names no owner, the same reading Adaptatron's "you may kill a gear" takes; scoped to the battlefield because `[Shield]` contributes only while defending, so it does nothing at all to a unit outside this fight. (3) **"[Shield 2] this combat" is implemented as this TURN** — it lands on `keywordsThisTurn`, so a second combat in the same turn would still find the Shield. There is no per-combat keyword scope in this engine and inventing one for a single card would be a subsystem. Reaver's Row's "move a friendly unit here to base" goes through `relocateToBaseUnchanged`, so the unit does NOT exhaust and `movesThisTurn` is untouched: the card says move, and 454's Recall is the thing that leaves statuses alone while this engine's `recallUnitToBase` force-exhausts for the player-initiated retreat |
| Battlefield abilities: the six CONTINUOUS ones | Trifarian War Camp (+1 Might here), Windswept Hillock (`[Ganking]` here), Vilemaw's Lair (no moving from here to base), Void Gate (+1 Bonus Damage to units here), Aspirant's Climb (points to win +1) and Bandle Tree (an extra facedown card here) | **Implemented 2026-08-04** in `engine/battlefield-continuous.ts`, read at each GATE rather than resolved as an effect — the split `board-restrictions.ts` already exists for. `effective-might`, `effectiveKeywords`, `dealDamage`'s modifier, the recall enumerator AND validator, the hide enumerator AND validator, and both readers of the Victory Score | 477 / 811 / 474 | **Four of the six read sites are enumerator/validator PAIRS**, which is the whole hazard: each asks one shared function, because the two disagreeing is how this codebase has produced three offered-then-refused bugs. Four readings, all **Unverified**. (1) **"Units here" is BOTH sides.** The War Camp and the Hillock name no owner, unlike every unit aura in `effective-might.ts`, so they help the opponent's units standing there too. (2) **Vilemaw's Lair blocks the RecallUnit action AND card-driven "move to base"** (Flash, Maddened Marauder — both say *move*, which is what the Lair forbids), and does NOT block combat's own step-3d recall: `relocateToBaseUnchanged` is a step of the Combat Cleanup (466) rather than a move a player makes, and blocking it would strand the losing side's survivors at a battlefield the rules have just sent them home from. A card-driven move that finds itself blocked does as much as it can and no more (055); the spell still resolves. (3) **Void Gate does not touch COMBAT damage**, and that is structural rather than chosen — `combat.ts` does its own Might arithmetic and never comes through `dealDamage` — which happens to match the card, since it says "spells and abilities". It is applied BEFORE the lethal test, so the bonus can be what kills. (4) **Aspirant's Climb is the only one that is not about its own battlefield**: `constants.victoryScore(state)` replaces the bare `WIN_THRESHOLD_1V1` in `win-condition.winner`, in 471.1.b's Final Point rule and in `opponentNearVictory`, because a threshold that is right in one reader and stale in another is how a game ends a point early. The board's own pip row takes it as a prop for the same reason. **`WIN_THRESHOLD_1V1`'s comment used to say Aspirant's Climb "isn't a battlefield mechanic yet (battlefields currently carry no passive effects at all)"** — the doc-comment shape this codebase has learned to read as "here is a hook that does not exist" |
| Battlefield abilities: the last four, and the completeness gate | Obelisk of Power and The Arena's Greatest ("at the start of each player's first Beginning Phase"), Back-Alley Bar ("when a unit moves from here") and The Dreaming Tree ("when a player chooses a friendly unit here with a spell for the first time each turn") | **Implemented 2026-08-04. All 24 printed battlefields now do something**, pinned by `test/battlefield-coverage.test.ts` | 383 / 355 / 454 | Four readings, all **Unverified**. (1) **The Beginning-Phase pair resolve INLINE, not on the chain** — the same deliberate exception `beginningPhase` already is for Dr. Mundo, Mushroom Pouch and Jinx - Loose Cannon's Legend, and The Arena's Greatest is the card that makes it matter: holding it would gain the point AFTER `scoreHolds`, which is a point gained in the wrong phase. (2) **"Their FIRST Beginning Phase" is `turnNumber === 1`, and that is exact rather than approximate.** `runEnd` advances the counter only when play wraps back to the first player (118), so both opening turns are turn 1 and each player gets exactly one. The only thing that could give a player two Beginning Phases at turn 1 is an extra turn — and Time Warp costs 10 Energy plus 4 Power against a turn-1 pool of two or three runes, so it is unreachable rather than merely unlikely. Measured, not assumed. (3) **Back-Alley Bar rides the `unitMoved` site, so a RECALL does not pay it.** 456 says a Recall is not a Move, which is the same line `movesThisTurn` and the `unitMoved` event already draw — riding the site is what stops the three disagreeing. A unit leaving BASE reaches no battlefield ability, since `originId` is `"base"` and matches none. (4) **The Dreaming Tree fires at ANNOUNCE**, because 355 makes each chosen unit a target as the Spell goes on the Chain — so a unit moved or killed while the Spell waits was still chosen. One Pending Item per chosen unit; placed after the Spell, so under LIFO the draw resolves BEFORE the Spell it watched. Its "first time each turn" is a RESOURCE checked at resolution rather than a trigger condition, following Wraith of Echoes, and it is tracked as a LIST of battlefield ids per player (`spellChoiceDrawnBattlefieldIds`) because both players pick from their own pool and two Dreaming Trees really can be in play at once |
| The Grand Plaza: a win that is not a score | "You win the game" is a win condition in its own right (195) | `GameState.declaredWinnerIndex`, read by `win-condition.winner` before it compares scores | 195 | Deliberately NOT expressed as awarding enough points to reach the Victory Score: a player put on 8 would also satisfy every "while an opponent is within 3 points of the Victory Score" clause on the board (Leona - Zealot, Find Your Center), and `winner` refuses a TIE at or above the threshold, so a points-shaped win could be denied by an opponent already on 8. Set once and never cleared — a win does not lapse |
| Sett's Recall on a save | — | `heal it, exhaust it, and recall it` puts the unit in base with damage cleared, exhausted, and its buff spent | 454 / 702.2.b | Conformant in substance and listed here only because the buff removal has two possible readings: it is charged as part of the printed COST ("spend its buff"), not as rule 705's leave-play cleanup, since a saved unit never leaves play. Both readings produce the same board |
| A unit-valued ADDITIONAL COST, on a SPELL | 204 has an additional cost paid as the card is PLAYED, alongside its printed cost, before it goes on the Chain | **Paid at RESOLUTION**, inside the card's own `resolve` | 204 / 204.2.a | **Shared by all seven unit-valued additional costs, recorded once against the set rather than per card** — Cruel Patron (OGN-208), Call to Glory (OGN-207), Wallop (OGN-146), Wildclaw Shaman (OGN-147), Kraken Hunter (OGN-150), Commander Ledros (OGN-231), and now Sacrifice (UNL-173). The chosen unit is fixed at ANNOUNCE — it rides `additionalCostUnitInstanceId` on the action, and both `legal-actions` and `validate-play-card` check its eligibility there — so what is late is only the payment's EXECUTION, not the choice. Reachable only on the spells: a unit's cost is paid the moment it enters play, which is when it is due. The observable gap is that between announcing a Sacrifice and resolving it, a response could kill or move the unit named as its price, and the cost then silently goes unpaid instead of the play being illegal. **Deliberately not fixed for one card**: moving Sacrifice alone would put it out of step with six shipped cards, and one reading applied consistently beats two readings of one rule. Closing it means a shared pay-on-announce step, which is a change to the play pipeline rather than to any card. **A second consequence surfaced on 2026-08-12, and it is the one that makes this gap concrete: Stalking Wolf's (UNL-166) placement clause is INERT.** "You may play me to its battlefield (even if you don't have other units there)" exists because, under 204, the meal is already dead when he is placed and he therefore has nobody there. Here the meal is still standing, supplies presence itself, and the ordinary reinforce rule carries the play — **measured by deleting `PLACEMENT_GRANTS["UNL-166"]` entirely and watching every test stay green**. The observable behaviour is identical either way, so the card is not half-written; the waiver is kept because it becomes load-bearing the moment the timing is fixed, and is pinned by asking `mayPlaceWithoutPresence` directly in `stalking-wolf.test.ts`. The cost-unit argument threaded into `validate-play-card` for it is correct and **cannot be killed by mutation today** for the same reason — the branch needing it is unreachable. |
| A delayed trigger whose subject leaves the board and comes back | 124: "A Game Object that changes zones to or from a Non-Board Zone becomes a new object for the purposes of tracking that object", and 390.5.a closes a Delayed Linked Ability's window when its object leaves an appropriate zone. So Deadly Flourish's "when IT dies this turn" must not fire for a card that was bounced, recycled or replayed in between | **A mark on the victim's `abilityModesUsedThisTurn`, stamped with `turnNumber` and `activePlayerIndex`**, read by a death-watch firing from the caster's trash. `turn-manager`'s `expireMightThisTurn` sweeps the mark off base units and battlefield units only, so a victim that reached a NON-BOARD zone keeps it — and the two-part stamp is what makes the stale copy fail to match | **124**, **390.2**, **390.5.a**, with **808.1.d.3** for the snapshot the mark rides on | **DIVERGENT in ONE case, recorded 2026-08-12 with UNL-073 Deadly Flourish.** Closed for the ordinary routes: a later turn changes the stamp, and the mark is stripped off the corpse once it has paid so a same-turn replay out of the trash (Last Rites) cannot pay twice. **What remains open is an EXTRA TURN** — Time Warp repeats both `turnNumber` (which counts rounds, not turns) and `activePlayerIndex`, so a victim marked on the first of two consecutive turns, bounced to hand, and replayed and killed on the second would pay a Gold it should not. Wrong in the GENEROUS direction, which is why it is recorded rather than papered over. The real fix is a zone-change hook that clears this-turn instance state per 124.1 — that is `effect-helpers.ts`'s `returnUnitToHand` / `completeDeath` / `recycleUnitFromPlayToDeck`, not a card file, and it would retire the stamp entirely. Deliberately NOT pinned by a test: the setup needs Time Warp, a bounce and a replay in one line, and a pin nobody can read is worse than this row |
| `[Repeat]`, more than one instance on one spell | 820.3 — each instance paid separately, and the instructions execute an additional time FOR EACH instance paid | **IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-14 for PRINTED instances.** `REPEAT_COSTS` holds a LIST, `PlayCardAction.repeatExecutions` names which instances were paid and carries each execution's own choices, and `card-effect-resolution` runs one execution per entry. UNL-182 Curtain Call is the card that made it reachable: all seven non-empty subsets of its three instances are offered, at seven prices, and paying all three executes four times. `EffectDefinition.distinctModesPerExecution` is its "choose one you haven't already chosen", which is the pool's only printed exception to 820.2.a's default. Pinned in `test/curtain-call-repeat.test.ts`. **The GRANTED half is still a boolean** — see the Syndra row above, which is now the only part of this open | 820.1.c.2 / 820.1.c.3 / 820.3 / 820.3.a | **Was: NOT a divergence yet — unreachable, and NO ruling is needed.** The rules settle every part of it: each Cost is paid or not individually (820.1.c.2), each only once (820.1.c.3), one extra execution per instance paid (820.3), choices may differ per execution (820.2.a), and the spell is still PLAYED once however many times it executes (820.3.a — which is what keeps `[Legion]` and `cardsPlayedThisTurn` right). **Two cards make it reachable and neither is written.** Curtain Call (UNL-182) prints three costs — `[Repeat] — [1] / [rainbow] / [1][rainbow]` — and its own reminder says "You may pay EACH additional cost", so they are three instances and up to FOUR executions; it also prints "Choose one you haven't already chosen", a per-execution constraint the single `modeId` on an action cannot express. Syndra - Transcendent (UNL-146) GRANTS `[Repeat] [2][Chaos]` to your spells while she is in a showdown, so casting Existential Dread (UNL-134, which prints its own) beside her is the first two-instance case from two different sources. The work is a LIST of costs payable individually plus a choice set per execution — not a reading. |
| "Move ... to a single location" and the enemy base | 198.1 makes a Base a Location and 355.4.a makes any Location the unit may occupy a valid Move Destination | **Settled: it DOES include the enemy base** | 198.1 / 355.4.a / 107.1.c | **Project-owner ruling, 2026-08-13.** Raised by a wave-8 agent against Tricksy Tentacles (UNL-054), which is the only card in the pool that phrases a destination this way. The engine models a base per controller and every target of that card shares a controller, so "their base" is well defined. Consequence for whoever writes it: the card needs a `MOVE_TO_BASE_DEF_IDS` row as well as a `MOVE_TARGET_SPELL_DEF_IDS` one, and the second is not sufficient alone — `withDestinations`' `toBase` branch is gated on an index derived from the single `targetUnitInstanceId` that a `unitList` play never sets, so the base would silently never be offered. That gate is read, not measured; measure it when the card lands. |

## How Burn Out was found, and what it says about the other gaps

Worth recording as a method, not just a fix. Burn Out sat in the Divergent table
for a long time with an explicit note that it was "a genuine gap, just not a
demonstrated liveness bug" — 40/40 self-play terminated, so nothing pointed at
it.

It was demonstrated by accident. Re-tuning the AI's `cardInHand` weight made the
AI spend cards faster, which reached two empty decks, which nothing could
resolve: self-play sat at score 7-7 with both decks empty and no battlefield
held, passing back and forth to **turn 538**. The rule that breaks that position
is the one that was missing.

Two things follow. First, "our probes terminate" is evidence about the
DISTRIBUTION the probes explore, not about the engine — a change in how the AI
values anything can move that distribution onto a gap that was always there.
Second, a tuning run is a liveness probe whether or not it is meant to be, and
is worth reading as one. The remaining Divergent rows below have not been
cleared by 40/40 either; they have only not been visited.

## Readings taken for the Stun and Legend pass

Nineteen cards landed together and each of these is a judgement, not a
mechanical port. Recorded so the next reader can overrule one without having to
rediscover that a choice was made at all.

| Card | The reading | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Stun as an event | ONE `unitsStunned` event per instruction, carrying every unit that actually became stunned, rather than one event per unit | **Conformant**, and forced by the cards: Leona - Radiant Dawn pays out once for "one or more enemy units" while Eclipse Herald triggers per "an enemy unit". A per-unit event cannot express the first. Rule 423's "a Stunned Unit can not be Stunned again" is why an already-stunned unit is dropped from the batch — the rules use Eclipse Herald as their own worked example of that |
| "Stun a unit" vs "a unit at a battlefield" | Rune Prison, Solari Shieldbearer and Miss Fortune's grant reach a unit in EITHER base; Zenith Blade's enemy target and Facebreaker's pair do not | **Conformant** 355.9.b — the bare noun means objects on the Board, and Bases are Public. Same reading Final Spark, Discipline and Stupefy already carry |
| Facebreaker's "same battlefield" | A relation between the two chosen targets, enforced during ENUMERATION and re-checked in the validator, not inside the resolver | **Conformant.** A resolver check would come too late: the card would already be paid for. First `TargetingSpec` in this engine whose targets constrain each other (`sameBattlefield`) |
| Zenith Blade's two scopes | The enemy target is "at a battlefield", the friendly one is not — so the spec carries a per-slot scope (`slotScopes`) | **Conformant** 355.9.b, applied twice with different answers. Reading one scope for both would either forbid sending the unit you most want to send (the one at home) or make the enemy targetable in their own base |
| Twisted Fate / Leona - Determined / Yasuo - Remorseful / Teemo - Strategist auto-selected targets | Auto-selected in stable board order rather than offered as a choice | **Unverified, and its stated REASON is now stale — the simplification survives, the excuse does not.** This used to read "an on-attack trigger fires inside the move/play executor with no action left to hang a choice on". Since 2026-08-03 it does not: Attack Triggers are Chain Pending Items, held and finalized by the Cleanup, so there IS a moment to ask in and the blocker is now the narrower one this file records under **Pending vs. Finalized chain items** — nothing chooses targets between Pending and Finalized for ANY held trigger, so these cards pick at resolution like every other one. The PDF's own worked example for Yasuo is exactly the case that needs the missing step: it has the trigger TARGETING as it goes on the chain and then MISTARGETING if the board moves under it. Ava Achiever is the counter-example proving the difference is targets and not choices in general — she parks a real decision, because a card in hand plus a payment is something `decisions.ts` can carry and a TARGET is not |
| Anivia - Primal / Warwick - Hunter sweep the battlefield | "All enemy units here" and "all damaged enemy units here" — no target is chosen at all | **Conformant.** Neither card names a choice, so none of the auto-selection caveat above applies to them. Warwick's "damaged" reads MARKED damage (`damage > 0`), which Combat Cleanup heals at 466 step 3c — so he is a follow-up punisher and finds nothing on a freshly-healed board, and he kills through `destroyUnit` so each death fires its own `[Deathknell]` |
| Stormbringer's damage-then-move order | Damage resolves while the unit is still in BASE, then it deploys | **Conformant** — printed order, and it is the card: the unit is not among "all enemy units at a battlefield" from either side, so it takes nothing back and arrives undamaged into the fight it started. Might is read ONCE before the damage, so the first kill's Deathknell cannot change what the rest take |
| Last Stand / Yasuo / Stormbringer read EFFECTIVE Might | Auras and this-turn pumps count; `[Assault]`/`[Shield]` do not (`isCombat: false`) | **Unverified.** Precedent: Gentlemen's Duel's "damage equal to their Mights". For Yasuo it is also anti-double-count — `combat.ts` applies `[Assault]` separately in the damage step of the same fight |
| Herald of Scales stacks | Two Heralds are -4, not -2 | **Unverified.** 817.1.a's redundancy rule is about KEYWORDS, and a continuous ability is not one; the precedent here is effective-might's Garen - Commander and Darius - Executioner, which explicitly both apply. The printed floor of 1 is unreachable in this pool — all 8 Dragons cost 5+ Energy — so it is pinned on a raw cost rather than on a real card's |
| Albus Ferros may spend a buff for nothing | Spending with an empty rune deck is allowed and yields no rune | **Unverified**, and the most arguable call in the sweep. The text is not "[do X] to [do Y]" cost phrasing, so it reads as an instruction rather than a cost, and 416.3's "must be able to be completed" therefore does not gate it |
| King's Edict: the CASTER kills | "Each other player chooses… **Kill those units**" splits choosing from killing | **Conformant.** The contrast is Cull the Weak's "each player *kills* one of their units", where the chooser is the killer. Measured rather than asserted: a Solari Shrine ("when **you** kill a stunned enemy unit") on the caster's board fires for the caster |
| Two slots may share a ROLE without sharing a MEANING | `asymmetricSlots` opts a `unitSlots` spec out of the mirror-ordering pruning | **Conformant.** `legal-actions` collapses (A,B) and (B,A) when both slots take the same role — right for Back to Back and Singularity, which do the same thing to each unit, and wrong for Convergent Mutation, whose slot 0 is the beneficiary and slot 1 is only measured. Measured before the fix: with a 7-Might and a 2-Might friendly, the ONLY ordering offered was the one that increases by 0. Opt-IN, so every other two-slot card keeps the pruning that halves the AI's search space, with Singularity as the control test |
| Soulgorger's trash affordability under-offers | `payPowerFromChanneled` takes one domain and reads only `channeled` | **DIVERGENT, conservatively.** A trashed unit with a `powerDomainAlt` split pip (Tibbers) is judged on its primary domain only, and floating Power is not counted. Strictly withholds an option, never grants one free. Widening needs a second domain parameter on the helper |
| Twisted Fate's dead domains | A Calm, Body or Chaos rune does nothing at all | **Conformant** — the card prints three branches and no default. Inventing a fallback would make it strictly better than it reads |
| Solari Shrine's "when YOU kill" | `DeathContext.killerIndex`, threaded from `dealDamage`'s caster, `destroyUnit`'s new parameter, and combat's opposing side | **Conformant.** Without a killer, neither "you" nor "enemy" has an answer and the Shrine would fire for its own controller's units being cleaned up. Left OPTIONAL so an unattributed death stays expressible: `[Temporary]` expiring (816) kills a unit with nobody behind it |
| Leona - Zealot's "-8 Might, minimum 1" | Applied after every other Might term and clamped, rather than summed as a bonus | **Conformant.** A floor is not a delta, and ordering it last is what makes it mean what it says: an aura arriving alongside her lifts the unit before the clamp. First enemy-side continuous modifier in the engine, and the first with a floor |
| Yasuo's "to or from its base" | Two modes rather than one compound unit+battlefield target | **Conformant in effect.** Going home names only a unit; leaving home also names a destination, so they are genuinely two target shapes. `modesOncePerTurn` is deliberately NOT set — his exhaust already caps him at once, unlike Udyr |
| Darius's `[Legion]` | A condition on the EFFECT, not on the cost — an unmet [Legion] still spends the exhaust and yields nothing | **Conformant** to the printed wording. Read from `cardsPlayedThisTurn`, which already existed |
| Kai'Sa's rainbow Power | A `restrictedSpellPower` scalar on PlayerState, drained after `floatingPower` and only for Spells | **Conformant**, mirroring the `restrictedSpellEnergy` pool Lux - Crownguard already needed. A scalar rather than a `floatingPower` entry because that record is keyed by Domain and this Power is rainbow — a seventh pseudo-domain would have to be understood, and ignored, by every consumer |
| Volibear's `[Mighty]` | Asked of the unit's CURRENT Might via the same `isMighty` Fiora - Victorious uses | **Conformant** 711. Reading printed Might would disagree with the board whenever an aura is present |

## Readings taken for the three permanent events (2026-08-02)

`endOfTurn`, `unitReadied` and `battlefieldHeld` — the last three events the
remaining card pool asked for. Each is a judgement rather than a mechanical port,
and each is recorded so the next reader can overrule it without rediscovering
that a choice was made.

| Card / mechanic | The reading | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Pirate's Haven includes the AWAKEN | "When you ready a friendly unit" covers the Awakening Phase's mass ready, so it is +1 Might to every exhausted unit you control, every turn | **Unverified, and it is the whole power level of the card.** The rules answer is unambiguous — 415: *"A player Readies all non-spell Game Objects they Control during the Awakening Phase on their turn"*, so the Awaken IS a readying performed by the player. Whether a card this broad was intended is a design question the rules do not answer. Implemented as printed. Settled in `docs/rules-calls-resolved.md` §8 |
| A ready is only a ready if the unit was EXHAUSTED | `readyUnit` no-ops on an already-Ready unit and fires no event | **Conformant** 415: *"A Unit that is already Ready cannot be Readied again. If a Unit is instructed to be Readied while it is already Ready, nothing additional happens."* Same guard shape as `addBuff` (702.3.a) and `stunUnits` (423). It is what stops Pirate's Haven from pumping a standing board for free, and it is enforced in the helper rather than in the card so no future ready effect can miss it |
| `unitReadied` carries no READIER | Only the readied unit's owner, so "when **you** ready" and "a **friendly** unit" collapse into one check | **Conformant in this pool, and named as an assumption.** All thirteen `readyUnit` call sites ready the actor's own unit — each is "ready me" or an `owner: "friendly"` target — and the Awaken readies the active player's own board. A carried readier would be the owner at every site: a field that cannot be wrong yet, which is the kind that goes wrong silently later. The day a card readies an ENEMY unit, that is the field to add |
| `battlefieldHeld` is the SCORING moment, not mere presence | Fired from `scoreHolds`, so a battlefield already scored this turn by a Conquer fires nothing | **Conformant** 469.2 (*"maintains Control of a Battlefield they did not yet Score this turn"*) and 470 (one score per battlefield per turn, from either method). Reading it as "still has units here" would make Ahri - Alluring pay out on a battlefield she had just conquered, which 470 forbids scoring at all |
| Ahri - Alluring DOUBLES a hold | The ordinary hold point is awarded by `scoreHolds`, and her "you score 1 point" is a second one on top | **Conformant.** Fired after the points are recorded, so she reads the score her own hold produced. A plain `points + 1`, deliberately not routed through `recordConquest` — rule 471.1.b's Final Point restriction applies only to a point gained "through a Conquer", and that path would silently withhold a winning point |
| Blitzcrank bounces AFTER the point | "When I hold, return me to my owner's hand" does not cancel the hold it triggered on | **Conformant**, and it is the card: a 5-Might `[Tank]` that trades itself for a point and comes back to be replayed. `returnUnitToHand`, not a recall — "to my owner's HAND" is leaving play, so 705 strips the buff and the next play is a fresh on-play trigger |
| Blitzcrank's grab is a QUESTION, not a target | "You may move an enemy unit to here" parks a decision at resolution rather than taking a target on the action | **Divergent, conservatively**, and the same shape Adaptatron's "you may kill a gear" already takes. The rules choose targets when the ability is finalized on the chain; this asks at resolution. A target on the action would have made the grab compulsory whenever any enemy unit existed, and the card is frequently better declined — dragging a body onto your own battlefield is how you lose the hold his third clause is built around |
| "An enemy unit" reaches their BASE | Blitzcrank can drag a unit out of the opponent's base, not only off a battlefield | **Conformant** 355.9.b's bare-noun reading, and the same one Rune Prison and Solari Shieldbearer already take. It is also the interesting half of the card, since pulling a defender off a battlefield is what Charm already does |
| Sona - Harmonious readies the maximum | "Ready up to 4 friendly runes" readies as many as it can, in pool order, with no choice offered | **Conformant in effect.** Readying is strictly beneficial and never wrong, so maxing it out is the faithful implementation rather than a shortcut — the same call Annie - Dark Child's "up to 2" already made, and the two now share one `readyRunes` in `effect-helpers.ts` rather than two copies of "up to" |

## Readings taken for the keyword auras (2026-08-02)

The pool's four "other friendly units have [X]" cards, landed together because
they are one table. Rule **477** puts them in layer 2, "Ability-Altering
Effects", identified by the words *have*/*has* — and the PDF's own worked example
for that layer is one of these very sentences.

| Card / mechanic | The reading | Status |
|---|---|---|
| An aura is keyed by its SOURCE | `KEYWORD_AURAS` is a second table beside `CONDITIONAL_GRANTS`, not more entries in it | **Conformant.** Every entry in the existing table is a card talking about ITSELF ("while I'm buffed, I have [Ganking]"), so it is keyed by the receiving unit's defId. An aura has to be looked up from the other end, which that shape cannot express |
| "OTHER" is a different OBJECT, not a different card | The self-exclusion compares `instanceId`, never `defId` | **Conformant, and it was wrong first.** Two Captain Farrons at one battlefield each satisfy the other's "other friendly units", so each has [Assault] and neither grants it to itself — a defId comparison gets that exactly backwards and excludes both. Farron is an ordinary unit, three to a deck, so this is an ordinary board rather than an edge case |
| "Here" reaches nobody from BASE | A positional aura whose source stands in base grants nothing | **Conformant** — a base is not a battlefield, so no source can share one with the unit. Same reading, for the same reason, as Lee Sin - Centered's and Leona - Zealot's positional Might auras |
| Spirit's Refuge's "if they didn't already" | Non-stacking, expressed as `Math.max` rather than as a condition | **Conformant.** It is what stops the granted `[Deflect 1]` LOWERING Volibear - Furious's printed `[Deflect 2]` — the direction that would be invisible, since the tax would simply be smaller |
| **Gemcraft Seer's aura is not inert** | "Other friendly units have [Vision]" makes every OTHER unit predict as it enters | **Conformant, and settled by the keyword's own definition rather than guessed.** Read as a "when you PLAY me" ability the grant would do nothing, because every unit already on the board has had its play moment. But 818's Vision entry says *"It is functionally short for 'When this is played, predict.' **The trigger is the permanent entering the Board**"* — so a unit arriving while she stands has the keyword at the moment the trigger condition is met. The PDF also uses her exact sentence as its layers example |
| `[Vision]` fires from the funnel, not from two card entries | `dispatchOnPlayUnit` applies it for any unit that has the keyword | **Conformant, and a structural fix.** It was two `UNIT_TRIGGERS` entries (Mystic Poro, Sai Scout) whose whole body was `applyVision`, plus a hardcoded `VISION_UNIT_DEF_IDS` set of the same two. A set keyed by the printed card can never see a granted one, so the aura would have been silently inert — the card resolves, the unit arrives, nothing errors. The enumerator, the validator AND the web board now ask one board-aware function; the compiler named all three when its signature changed |
| Multiple instances of `[Vision]` | A Mystic Poro played beside a Gemcraft Seer predicts ONCE | **DIVERGENT.** 818: *"Multiple instances of Vision trigger separately… The player may choose to recycle or not recycle for each instance separately."* `effectiveKeywords` returns a VALUE per keyword rather than a COUNT, and `visionRecycle` is a single boolean on the action. Right for the valued keywords — two sources granting [Shield] is still [Shield 1], per 817.1.a's redundancy rule — and wrong for this one. Fixing it needs a count on the action or a decision queue; pinned by a test so it is a known number rather than an accident |
| Captain Farron never had `[Assault]` | The loader was reading his own grant as a printed keyword | **Was a live bug.** `parseKeywords` sees brackets and cannot tell a card claiming a keyword from a card handing one out, so Farron shipped swinging +1 as an attacker on a card that says nothing of the sort. Third instance of this shape after `HIDDEN_KEYWORD_FALSE_POSITIVES` and `CONDITIONAL_KEYWORD_DEF_IDS`; the strip is `GRANTED_ONLY_KEYWORDS` and it is per-KEYWORD, because **Taric - Protector prints `[Shield]` AND grants `[Shield]`** in one text and a per-card strip would take his real one with it. Measured across the pool rather than assumed: four cards grant a bracketed keyword, exactly two are false positives |

**Layers are a real rules concept this engine does not model, and mostly does not
need to.** 477 defines five ordered layers (Trait-Altering → Ability-Altering →
Arithmetic → …) with re-evaluation between them; this engine recomputes
everything on read, which lands on the same answer for every card in the pool and
is what the PDF's own Fiora - Victorious example describes. The one place the
difference is visible is **snapshotting** — an arithmetic effect from a
non-passive source is limited at application time and remembered at that level —
which nothing here implements. Named now because it is the frame the next
Might-altering card will need.

## Traps that were filed for the timing work — all resolved

Kept as a record of what the trap list was for, since all four were real and
each would have been a silent mistake. Traps 1–3 are fixed; trap 4 came true
exactly as predicted and is now a tracked Divergent row rather than a warning.

1. ~~`isAction` never reaches the runtime~~ — `createCardInstance` now copies it
   onto `SpellInstance`. Pinned by a test that reads it off a real instance.
2. ~~Reaction implies Action, but the loader sets `isAction: false` for a
   `[Reaction]`-only card~~ — the tier is now *derived* (`timing.timingTierOf`)
   rather than the flags being tested at each call site. Pinned by a test
   asserting Gust is `{isReaction: true, isAction: false}` and still tier
   `Reaction`; reading `isAction` alone would have barred all 8 Reaction spells
   from Showdowns.
3. ~~`legal-actions`' Showdown branch already enumerates for `focusHolder` —
   extend that concept~~ — extended and extracted: `timing.actingPlayerIndex` is
   now the single definition, replacing three copies. It also fixed a latent
   ordering bug: the old code tested `turnState === "Showdown"` *before*
   `!chainOpen`, which would have enumerated for the Focus holder when rule 313
   gives priority to the chain.
4. **The AI's lookahead assumes the opponent doesn't respond** — came true. Now
   a Divergent row above, with the 2-ply fix named as its own piece of work.

Two more worth recording, both found only by running the thing:

- **Enumeration and validation must share every gate, not just the tier.** The
  813 destination rule lived only in the validator at first, so `legalActions`
  offered a `[Reaction]` Unit a reinforce destination the validator then refused
  — and the AI, which trusts `legalActions` and calls executors directly, threw
  mid-game. Both now call `timing.mayPlayUnitToBattlefield`.
- **Anything that applies actions outside `submit` must run the Cleanup too.**
  `heuristic-ai.applyAction` called executors bare, so once Showdowns were
  staged in the Cleanup the AI could no longer see one open — it would have
  stopped walking onto empty battlefields entirely, and looked like a bad
  evaluator rather than a missing cleanup.

## Unverified (the honest bulk)

Everything else, including all 93 oracle-mirrored behaviours. Highest-value
targets for the next pass, by blast radius:

0. **OPEN, raised 2026-08-13 — in what ORDER does a replaced base cost compose
   with a base-cost reduction?** 356.1 says "Apply base cost modifications in any
   order" and 356.1.a makes "play this for [Cost]" one of them — but replacement
   and subtraction do not commute, so "any order" cannot be taken literally when
   both apply. This engine replaces FIRST and then reduces, which is the reading
   that makes a reduction still worth something on a replaced cost; the other
   reading (reduce the print, then discard it for the replacement) makes the
   reduction inert. **No card in the pool reaches the case**: the two cards with
   replaced costs (UNL-025, UNL-089) are untouched by every discount currently
   written, so the choice is presently unobservable and is recorded here rather
   than pinned by a test that would assert an invented interaction. Revisit when
   a third card makes it reachable. `engine/replaced-costs.ts` states the choice
   at its `ReplacedCost` doc comment.

1. **SETTLED 2026-08-06 — all three SFD rules calls are closed, and all three cards
   are implemented.** Raised by the agents that refused to write them rather than
   pick quietly, which is the reason each got a real answer instead of a guess:
   - **Tianna Crownguard (SFD-060)**: project-owner ruling — a blocked point does
     **not** unrecord the scoring, so 470's lockout still fires and the
     opponent cannot retry that battlefield this turn. Nine inline point-writes
     were funnelled through one `gainPoints` choke point to make the check
     reachable at all. `tianna-crownguard.test.ts`.
   - **Renata Glasc - Industrialist (SFD-171)**: settled **from the rules PDF, not
     by ruling** — see the Verified row. She overrides the printed “exhausted”,
     and reaches gear tokens as well as unit tokens. The intuitive answer
     (“explicit instruction beats blanket grant”) is wrong, and the four-step
     citation chain is written out in `board-restrictions.ts` so it is not
     re-litigated. `renata-industrialist.test.ts`.
   - **Riposte (SFD-206)**: first shipped with a resolution-time unit choice and
     a recorded divergence; **reworked the same day to the printed behaviour** on
     the project owner's call, so the divergence is closed rather than accepted.
     The `chainSpellAndUnit` targeting kind now carries both targets through
     announce. See the Verified row. `riposte.test.ts`.
2. **SETTLED 2026-08-06 — OGN's ten dual-domain Signature spells and OGS-024
   WERE split pips, and are fixed.** They were found as CONTROLS while
   confirming SFD's fourteen, which is the only reason anyone looked. Read at
   4x beside a single-domain control (Anivia, Body, 2 Power — one SOLID orange
   capsule with two Body glyphs), all eleven show the same left/right
   two-colour capsule Tibbers has. `card-loader`'s own note said the opposite
   of one of them ("Decisive Strike's pip is a solid single color and is NOT
   hybrid"); that note is corrected and the two tests pinning it are flipped.
   **No probe moved**, and that is worth stating rather than glossing: the fix
   only ever WIDENS what a rune can pay, so preset self-play cannot see it —
   its effect is visible to a player holding the other domain's runes and to
   nobody else. Eleven cards in two declared-complete sets were demanding their
   whole Power cost in one domain while half their pip said otherwise.
3. **Showdown/Combat step order** — rule 463's steps, HOT FEPR, and when the
   Attacker/Defender designations attach. Two divergences already found here.
4. **Costs and payment** — floating resources, rune recycling, exhaust-vs-recycle
   (rule 430 and neighbours).
4. **Triggered abilities** — the patch notes changed Attack/Defend triggers to
   *once per combat, the first time* (a change from prior FAQ guidance). We
   fire per unit on move/play into a contested battlefield; unverified.
5. **Does Hold mean Control, or occupation?** Rules 315.2.b.2 ("The Turn
   Player Holds all Battlefields they **Control**") and 469.2 ("maintains
   Control of a Battlefield they did not yet Score this turn") both define Hold
   by *Control*. `scoring.isHeldBy` instead requires the player to have units
   present AND no opponent units there. Implementing the control lapse (323.6
   step 4) closed most of the gap — in an Open State, Control now implies
   occupation — but the "no opponent units" half is still a real potential
   divergence: a contested battlefield you control but that an enemy unit is
   standing on scores under the rules' wording and not under ours. Left alone
   deliberately rather than changed on judgment: `isHeldBy` mirrors the Java
   oracle, switching it to read `controllerId` would let an abandoned
   battlefield score, and the lingering-contested state may simply be
   unreachable (Contested stages a Showdown, which re-establishes control).
   Needs a reachability check before anyone touches it.
6. **Does a card-driven recall exhaust?** Rule 454 says a Recall leaves
   statuses untouched, and Highlander spells out "exhaust it" precisely because
   the recall alone wouldn't. But `effect-helpers.recallUnitToBase` force-
   exhausts, and it backs **Flash** ("Move up to 2 friendly units to base") and
   **Maddened Marauder** ("move a unit from a battlefield to its base") — both
   of which say *move*, not *recall*, and neither mentions exhaustion. Combat
   cleanup's own recall was given a separate state-preserving helper rather
   than changing these on a guess. Also covers the player-initiated
   `RecallUnit` action (`execute-recall-unit.ts`), which exhausts too.
   **A THIRD card now rides it: Ezreal - Dashing (SFD-082)**, whose ability is
   ":rb_rune_mind:: [Action] — Move me to your base" — again *move*, again no
   mention of exhaustion. His ability itself takes no exhaust as a COST (none is
   printed), and that is a separate fact from the arrival exhaust this question
   is about; `test/sfd-mind.test.ts` asserts the cost shape and documents the
   arrival exhaust as this row rather than as his behaviour. He also makes the
   question slightly sharper: he is the first card where the recall is a
   REPEATABLE ability, so if the arrival exhaust is wrong, it is wrong every
   time the Power is available rather than once per spell.

## Card errata

Origins first batch: **31 cards**, of which **3** are in the four Proving
Grounds precons. Our card-data snapshot (`packages/engine/src/cards/*.json`)
was confirmed **post-errata** for all three on 2026-07-29:

| Card | Errata | Ours |
|---|---|---|
| Disintegrate | "If this kills it, **do this:** draw 1" | post-errata; implementation checks whether it actually died |
| Dune Drake | "+2 **this turn**" | post-errata; `bonus` is turn-scoped |
| Highlander | "the next time it **would** die … **heal it, exhaust it,** and recall it" | post-errata; `death-ward.ts` does heal → exhaust → recall |

The other 28 are outside our pool.

**Spiritforged errata is now IN scope and has not been checked.** This
paragraph used to read "not applicable, our card base is OGN + OGS", which
stopped being true on 2026-08-04 when `sfd.json` was loaded. The snapshot came
byte-for-byte from the frozen Java oracle and carries `metadata.updated_on`
stamps in 2026-03, i.e. after the set's 2026-02-13 release — so it is probably
post-errata, but that is an inference from a timestamp and not a check of any
errata list. Do the OGN-style confirmation before declaring SFD complete.

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| 2026-08-07 | **SFD IS COMPLETE — 198/198, and `"SFD"` is in `coverage.COMPLETE_SETS`.** `PARTIALLY_IMPLEMENTED` is empty for the first time since the set landed. The (A) decision is discharged: all 18 cards written, and the hard gate that names its cards now protects SFD as it has protected OGN and OGS. **`finishedButUndeclared` did exactly the job it was built for** — it fired the moment the count reached 198 and the suite said so, which is why the promotion was one line rather than something to remember. Svellsongur (SFD-059) landed on the human's call of "reachable subset plus a named divergence": every table where an ability can be doubled at all is done, and the two that cannot be reached cheaply are the row above rather than a partial note — the card's text IS implemented, and the gap is in how far a doubling reaches. **Three tests broke on the promotion and all three were fixed at the PREMISE, never the assertion**: the art-only list is empty, so the hand-kept loop was replaced by a sweep over all 31 Equipment; and `set-coverage`'s two scoping tests lost SFD as their in-progress subject, which the SYNTHETIC set `ZZZ` had been carrying all along — `coverageBySet` takes its complete-set list as an argument precisely so neither direction depends on a real set happening to be in the right state. |
| 2026-08-07 | **SFD reached 197/198 — every card but Svellsongur (SFD-059), and the price of that one is now MEASURED rather than estimated.** Ten of the eleven remaining cards landed, and **ten for ten of this repo's mechanism-naming notes were again wrong or stale**: `isFightingAt` cannot answer Vex's "while I'm in combat" (it takes a `GameEvent`; a cost is priced with none), Arise! prints no per-token destination clause at all, The Zero Drive's "banish-with-source tracking" is one optional field plus the death-watch that fills it, the Skyfall's "moment-rewriting layer with no precedent" is one function handing a trigger's own `applies` the other moment, Akshan's `[Body][Body]` half was said to already work and NOTHING was registered for the card, and Bard's `exhaustLegend` "kind with no id field" belongs in the boolean cost table rather than the one built to carry an id. Void Hatchling was priced honestly by the survey and cost about what it said. **Svellsongur is the one note that is right.** "Copy that unit's text to this Equipment" doubles the wearer's abilities — an Equipment's text is read as its wearer's, which the eight wearer's-moments cards establish — and a faithful copy has to reach every defId-keyed table: measured at **23 of them, over 256 units**. Some are free (a doubled keyword is redundant under 817.1.a; an on-play trigger cannot re-fire, since the attach happens after the play), some are cheap (event triggers, through the key list this session already built for Relentless Pursuit's grant), and two are not (continuous Might auras, 13 cards; cost modifiers, 11 cards) because each walks its own unit list and a gear is in none of them. Stopped and asked rather than landing a partial note, which is what the (A) decision requires. |
| 2026-08-07 | **The SFD end state is (A): all 18 remaining cards, and `"SFD"` in `COMPLETE_SETS`.** The decision was the human's and is recorded here because the two halves of the old definition of done could not both hold: `isCardImplemented` returns FALSE for any card carrying a `PARTIALLY_IMPLEMENTED` note (checked BEFORE the registry lookup), and `finishedButUndeclared` only fires at 198/198 — so while any partial note survived, SFD could not be declared complete and the hard gate would never protect it. Option (B) (finish 13, leave 5, sit at 193/198 ungated) was the previous plan's recommendation and was declined. **Two of the five "do not do" notes were also re-priced against the code and found overstated**: `equipment.ts` declares itself the single writer of `attachedToInstanceId`, so Brutalizer's "per-attachment turn stamp" is one field at one site; and `tags` is NOT printed-only — `card.ts` copies them onto every `UnitInstance` — so Experimental Hexplate's granted TAG is a readers-routing job, not a new subsystem. Both notes were believed for two sessions. Six for six of this repo's mechanism-naming notes have now been wrong or stale. |
| 2026-08-07 | **Last Rites (SFD-150) opened the engine's FOURTH play source and its first FULL-COST play from a non-hand zone, which is why it was done first.** Three cards were already written, correct, and inert: Void Drone (SFD-010) and Drag Under (SFD-164) discount "[2] less to play from anywhere other than your hand" and Rek'Sai - Breacher (SFD-029) grants `[Accelerate]` on the same condition — all keyed to `playedFromHand: false`, which only the Champion Zone could produce. The trash is now a second producer, so all three pay out on a real board. **The permission shape is a recorded divergence, not a shortcut**: 419.3.b wants the play performed inside the trigger's resolution with costs paid there, and this engine cannot — a play needs a `RunePayment` and `AnswerDecisionAction` carries only an `optionId`. Jayce - Man of Progress reached the identical shape by the identical argument a wave earlier. |
| 2026-08-07 | **Two of this card's own tests were written wrong first, in the exact shape trap 6 names, and the mutation is what exposed it.** The "leverage" tests originally asserted `modifiedEnergyCost(..., false)` — handing the rule its own precondition, so they tested the discount and not whether the trash reaches pricing as a non-hand play at all. Stubbing `mayPlayFromTrash` to `false` failed only 2 of 36. Rewritten to read the price the ENUMERATOR actually offers, the same stub fails 4 — and rewriting them immediately surfaced two real fixture bugs the function-level version could never have shown: `RunePayment` has no `runeIds` field, and Void Drone is FURY, so a Chaos-rune fixture made its `[Accelerate]` surcharge unpayable and the accelerated variant silently absent rather than wrong. A third test set `controllerIndex` on a battlefield, a field that does not exist; it passed anyway because `scoring.isHeldBy` reads UNIT PRESENCE, so the line was dead rather than load-bearing. Only the cross-workspace typecheck saw it — the suite was green. |
| 2026-08-07 | **The two card-keyed play restrictions point OPPOSITE ways, which is why only one of them is a `PLACEMENT_GRANTS` row.** Rengar - Pouncing (SFD-025) GRANTS a destination the ordinary rules forbid — "I can be played to a battlefield you're ATTACKING" — so he is one row and needs no new field: the Attacker designation is `contestedByIndex`, already on the battlefield, and it is asked through `attackerIndexAt`, the same function the "when I attack" triggers use, so a card that is PLAYED to an attack and a card that TRIGGERS on attacking cannot disagree about who is doing it. His `[Reaction]` timing is what makes the grant reachable at all: a battlefield is only one you are attacking while a Showdown is open. **Perched Grimwyrm (SFD-015) NARROWS instead**, and "(You can't play me anywhere else.)" makes the narrowing total — so BASE is refused too. That is precisely what `PLACEMENT_GRANTS` cannot express, since its default answer for a card with no entry is "the ordinary rules apply". It is a separate gate, asked by the enumerator and the validator alike; the base refusal is its own check because a base play carries no `destinationBattlefieldId` for the destination gate to see. |
| 2026-08-07 | **Grimwyrm reads `conqueredBattlefieldsThisTurn`, a NEW list, and deliberately not `scoredBattlefieldsThisTurn`.** The existing field is the neighbouring one and a different fact: it records the once-per-turn SCORING lockout (470), is written even when the point is withheld, and is also written by HOLDING — which is not conquering. The new list is recorded in `recordConquest`, where taking a battlefield actually happens, so a conquest whose point Tianna Crownguard withholds or whose scoring Forgotten Monument blocks still counts for him. |
| 2026-08-07 | **Guardian Angel (SFD-051) and Soraka - Wanderer (SFD-173) are FREE, MANDATORY death replacements, so they sit beside Zhonya's Hourglass in `killUnit` rather than going through `offerDeathReplacement`.** Neither prints "you may", so neither is a question and there is nothing to fold into the optional path. Their order relative to each other is stated in one function rather than falling out of two ifs: the Angel is checked first because it is the NARROWER card — it saves only its wearer and costs the gear — and letting Soraka's free save consume a death the Angel was attached for would waste it. Soraka's three clauses are each pinned separately, because a replacement that fires too widely reads as a unit that simply stopped dying: "ANOTHER" (she cannot save herself, by instance), "HERE" (nothing from base, nothing at another battlefield) and "LESS Might than me" (strictly less, so an EQUAL unit dies — the difference between "less" and "no more"). |
| 2026-08-07 | **The Guardian Angel's first implementation never fired, and the reason is an ordering the kill path documents for a different purpose.** `killUnit` runs `detachAllFrom` BEFORE any replacement is considered — it has to, since a dangling `attachedToInstanceId` reads in play as a Might bonus from an Equipment attached to nothing. So a live-board lookup for "a Guardian Angel attached to the dying unit" finds nothing by the time it runs. The fix is to take what the unit WAS wearing from the list `killUnit` already captures one line earlier, which is exactly what `PendingDeath.wornEquipment` exists for and says so in its own comment. | 2026-08-06 | **[Hidden]'s "next turn" was read as "next `turnNumber`", and it cost the FIRST player their entire [Hidden] window.** Reported from play: "I am unable to play cards from hidden — during the AI's turn / in a showdown". 811 (PDF line 3334) reads *"**Beginning on the next turn**, this gains [Reaction] and you may play this, ignoring its base cost"*, and 2027 restates it as "on any subsequent turn". But `turnNumber` counts ROUNDS — `turn-manager.runEnd` advances it only when play wraps back to the First Player — so `turnNumber > hiddenOnTurn` SKIPS the opponent's turn that falls between yours. Since a hidden card is played at Reaction speed, that skipped turn is the single moment it exists to be played in. **The bug was asymmetric, which is why it survived:** hide as the SECOND player and the round wraps the instant your turn ends, so `turnNumber` has already advanced and the old gate happened to be right; hide as the FIRST player and the card was dead until your own next turn. The gate now asks whether the owner's hiding TURN has ended — a later round, or the opponent's turn within the same one — which is sound because hiding is legal only on your own turn (`validate-hide-card` checks `activePlayerIndex`). **The existing test could not have caught it:** it simulated "the next turn" as a `turnNumber` bump, which is the buggy model itself. The new ones ask the question the way the game does, and both fail under mutation. |

Pinned by a test that kills the unit inside the response window and still expects the draw. (3) **76 tests broke, and every one was a premise about timing, not a regression.** Fixed by settling the chain — through a new `playUnitTrigger` fixture for the ~40 that call the dispatcher directly, and per-file for the rest. Worth keeping. (1) **Divine Judgment asks "which one goes", repeated until 2 remain, rather than "which two stay"** — the same outcomes with no accumulator, since a multi-select answer would have to be carried on the pending decision and validated against itself while a one-at-a-time cut is rebuilt from live state like every other question here. Recorded Unverified: the player names victims, not survivors. It RECYCLES rather than kills, which is what decides whether a [Deathknell] fires, and a rune recycles to the RUNE deck — the one place its four categories genuinely differ. (2) **Promising Future contains two different orderings in one sentence and they are not the same one**: the LOOK is APNAP, the PLAY is explicitly "starting with the next player", so the caster plays last. Both are implemented by parking order alone, and both are pinned by mutation. Its play step is a ONE-OPTION decision — `advanceDecisions` resolves it without ever prompting — which exists purely to be sequenced behind both looks; the queue is the only thing in this engine that can say "after both players have finished choosing". It is also the first card whose free play still charges the Power ("they must still pay Power costs"), so a player who cannot pay leaves it banished (422). (3) **Nocturne needed a funnel, not a table.** "As you look at or reveal me from the top of your deck" is spread across six unrelated card implementations, each slicing its own top-N, so a per-card table keyed by the LOOKING card could never see him — `engine/top-of-deck.ts` is the `unitMoved` shape again. Every caller passes the CARDS it looked at rather than a count, because half of them recycle those cards before anything can be answered and he may be at the bottom of the deck by the time his own offer resolves. Four callers park his offer FIRST (FIFO puts it ahead of their own question); two reveal-and-consume callers park it after. Recorded Unverified: banishing him shifts the looking effect's top-N up by one, so it sees a card the player never looked at. (4) **The coverage-drift invariant earned its keep within the hour** — Nocturne was registered only through a decision, exactly the half-written shape that test states, and the fix was a real coverage source (`top-of-deck looks`) rather than a suppression. It named exactly one site (`scoring.recordConquest`), which is the point: **an event kind has to be converted atomically at every producer**, because `holdEventTrigger` consults `applies` and `dispatchEvent` does not, so a half-converted kind resolves one way from one call site and the other way from another. Re-asking would let a trigger be cancelled by pushing its source one battlefield sideways. The conqueror check stays in both, since it reads only the event and the listener's owner and the window cannot change either. Adaptatron's "is there a gear to kill" check stays in `resolve` alone: that is a question about the board at resolution, not a trigger condition, and a trigger that fires and resolves to nothing is the rules working. **Ordering change, and it is a correction:** the conquest POINT (474) is now awarded before these resolve, where inline they ran first. No listener in this pool awards points, so nothing observable moves today.
