| 2026-08-02 | **Two documented protections turned out not to exist, and one board bug cost real points.** (1) **A Combat Showdown that one side leaves scored nothing.** `resolveShowdown` early-returned the state untouched whenever one side had no units — "nothing to fight". 466.3.a makes the only player with units remaining the WINNER; 466.3.d reserves "No Result" for step-3d recalls, both present, or neither present; 466.5.d makes Establishing Control a Conquer. So an opponent who Flashed a unit out of a Showdown (or whose last defender was killed mid-window) left the other player standing alone and uncredited. Measured over 200 preset games before → after: **points from a walkout 0 → 95**, control awarded 44 → 154 of 154 — zero before, so the bug was total, and it fires in ~3 of every 4 games. The damage exchange really is skipped (step 2 is conditional on both sides remaining) but everything after it runs, because 348.1 has Combat "proceed with the remaining steps" — so the Combat Cleanup and its global 3c heal happen too. **Two tests asserted the bug rather than a rule** and were rewritten, not weakened: a `result === state` "genuinely a no-op", and "an UNCONTESTED showdown heals nothing". `scratchpad/walkout.mjs` is the positive control and fails against the pre-fix engine. (2) **The board never masked anything.** Two rows here claimed `maskHiddenCards` was "applied at the AI's entry point **and the board's rendering**". It is not exported from `packages/engine/src/index.ts` at all — `typeof m.maskHiddenCards` on the built dist is `undefined` — so the web app cannot call it and never did. Behaviour is nonetheless correct: `BattlefieldView` withholds the opponent's facedown identity with a `mine` branch covering both label and tooltip, and the opponent's hand renders from `ai.hand.length` alone. But it is safe by two local rendering decisions, not by construction, and there is no test on either — a live probe over 400 steps never reached an enemy facedown card, so that branch is **unexercised**, not verified. Comments in `BattlefieldView` and `GameBoard` that asserted the state "is already masked" are corrected in place; they invited exactly the simplification that would leak. (3) **The mulligan screen overflowed at every width except the one it was built at.** `--card-h: clamp(200px, 52dvh, 480px)` is driven by viewport HEIGHT only and never asked whether four cards fit ACROSS: at 1280x800 four 298px cards plus gaps came to 1245px in a 1189px row, so one wrapped and `.card-row`'s `overflow-y: auto` hid it. Now capped on both axes against a `--board-content-w` published by `.board` itself, with the card count passed from the component rather than hardcoded to 4. Fixed at 1280x800, 1024x700 and 900x600; 1600x950 unchanged. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Settled the hold-then-flush model for triggers as Chain Pending Items, and converted the first dispatch site.** The blocker was that most of the 14 `dispatch*` entry points fire where no chain is open and nobody holds priority (Beginning Phase, Cleanup, scoring, mid-resolution, inside the decision queue), so a naive push would strand triggers. **Reading the rules PDF changed the framing rather than confirming it.** Memory and the old Divergent row cited "323 step 3a" as a general trigger flush; step 3a is **Deathknell-specific**. The actual model is 383 plus the chain's own loop (337-345): a trigger goes on the Chain the moment it fires, in any state, as a **Pending Item**, which is simply not respondable — priority is granted (345) only "if the Chain is not empty and there are **no Pending Items**". So the holding pen is not a workaround for the dispatch sites, it IS the Pending phase, and `runCleanup` is the Finalize step. `runCleanup` specifically because it is the only hook that runs after every action in **both** `submit` and the AI's lookahead; finalizing in `submit` would leave the lookahead scoring boards whose triggers never resolved, silently. Also corrected from the PDF: 383 makes turn order the **placement** order, so with LIFO resolution (340.1) the NON-turn player's triggers resolve first — `allListeningPermanents`' comment had asserted the opposite for months, harmlessly, because inline resolution makes placement and resolution the same event. **Prerequisites, each a no-op on its own:** rule 347 became reachable and needed `chainOpenedByTrigger` on GameState (it asks how the chain OPENED, not what just popped — a per-entry test would hand Focus away from the player rule 345 had just given it to); `legalActions` returned `[]` outside the Action phase, which a closed chain in the Beginning Phase would have turned into a hard hang; `settleDeferredResolution` answered decisions with `applyBare`, skipping the Cleanup, and its `MAX_SETTLE_PASSES = 16` both miscounted (every iteration costs one, not two per chain item) and failed **silently** — now 64 and it throws, matching `decisions.ts`'s precedent; the chain viewer skipped non-Spell entries, which would have rendered a closed chain as an empty one while demanding a PassFocus. **Converted `unitBuffed`** — one listener (Mistfall), tail position, and its resolution parks a question rather than moving the board. It immediately exposed a modelling gap: trigger CONDITIONS lived inside `resolve`, where "did not trigger" and "triggered and did nothing" are indistinguishable — true while inline, false once held, since holding one opens a real response window. Added an optional `applies` predicate to `EventTriggerDefinition`; without it, buffing an ENEMY unit opened a window for the opponent's Mistfall. Four Mistfall tests had also gone green for the wrong reason, asserting `pendingDecisions` was empty when nothing fires immediately after ANY buff now — they assert on the pen and on the settled state instead. Measured on the Sett buff deck, 300 games: max chain depth 3 → **6**, 1136 states with a trigger on the chain, 0 stranded pen, 300/300 terminated, 0 invalid; self-play 40/40, passive 16/16; tests 1130 → **1144**. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Fixed the Sett stranding — which turned out NOT to be the Chain divergence at all.** It had been recorded as the confirmed live symptom that made the Chain work a bug fix as well as a conformance fix. Measured: `killUnit` → `offerDeathReplacement` is a **replacement effect**, not a trigger, so none of the 14 conversions touch it. The real cause was two guards disagreeing about whether the game was over — `withCleanupAndWinnerCheck` correctly skips the winner check while a decision is pending (321), but `submit` gated on the bare points predicate `winner(state)` and so refused every later action **including the `AnswerDecision`** that would have finished the resolution. Rule 194.2 says a player wins "in a cleanup", so points over the threshold mid-resolution is not yet a win. One guard, one comment. **5 stranded units per 300 games → 0**, with all 5 won-while-pending answers now accepted. **The probe had the same bug**: it looped on `while (winner(state) === null)`, exiting before it could submit the answer, so it reported an unchanged 5 against fixed code — a fix that measured as no change at all. Rewritten to stop on the declared `result` like the UI does, it reports 5→0 and, run against the reverted engine, reproduces all 5 as `Invalid: "Game is already over"`. Regression test drives the real `submit` path; every pre-existing Sett test used `destroyUnit`/`answerDecisions` directly and bypassed the gate entirely. Separately repaired `ai-health.mjs`, the termination gate itself: it omitted `firstPlayerIndex`, so `turnNumber` never incremented and it had been reporting `turns: {min:1, median:1, max:1}` — a constant lie beside real numbers, which also meant `[Hidden]` (gated on `turnNumber > hiddenOnTurn`) was never exercised in any self-play run. Real games span 6–9 turns. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Call to Glory, and with it the imported Sett list reaches ZERO dead cards** — 35 working, 5 partial, 0 dead, the partial five being Fiora ×3 and Qiyana ×2 whose only gap is `[Deflect]`. "As you play this, you may spend a buff as an additional cost. If you do, ignore this spell's cost." The cost half is the first of its shape in this pool: paying the additional cost REPLACES the printed cost rather than adding to it, so affordability stopped being answerable once per card. `legal-actions` computed one payment per card and bailed on the printed cost before it ever built the paid variant, which made the zero-rune line — the whole point of the card — unreachable; it now prices per VARIANT (`ignoresCostWhenPaid` on `UnitCostSpec`, empty payment rather than a small one, mirroring rule 811's IGNORED-not-discounted shape), and `validate-play-card` re-derives the same zeroing. **This is a small, contained instance of exactly the restructure `[Deflect]` needs**, and it landed without incident — which lowers the estimate on that work. Cards doing nothing **90 → 89**; tests **1124 → 1130**. Self-play 40/40, passive 16/16, real-deck reachability 40 games 0 invalid with Kingpin now reaching **+11** over printed Might. Noted as a known simplification, shared with every optional cost here: the cost is paid at RESOLUTION rather than on announcement ("as you play this"), which only becomes observable once something can respond in between — i.e. once triggers are Chain Pending Items. |

| 2026-08-01 | **Landed the three `[Deflect]`-adjacent cards whose real text was implementable, and left the two that need new subsystems.** Having established that `[Deflect]` blocks nothing, the cards themselves were the work. **Deadbloom Predator** — "You may play me to an occupied enemy battlefield": the per-card placement boolean became a keyed table (`PLACEMENT_GRANTS`), because the pool now holds two near-opposite grants and a shared "has a grant" flag would let Sai Scout be played to Deadbloom's destination and vice versa — which for Deadbloom is not cosmetic, since arriving at an occupied enemy battlefield applies Contested and opens a Showdown. **Qiyana - Victorious** — "When I conquer, draw 1 or channel 1 rune exhausted": Kai'Sa - Survivor's `battlefieldConquered` shape (she must be AT the battlefield) plus a two-answer decision; both options are offered even when the matching pile is empty, since drawing from an empty deck is Burn Out (431) and therefore a real, sometimes-decisive choice, and `advanceDecisions` auto-resolves a one-option question so pruning would silently choose for the player. **Spirit's Refuge** — its "buff a friendly unit" half, as an on-play self-trigger plus a decision (Gear carries no targeting on the PlayCard action); its `[Deflect]`-granting sentence is deliberately not written, since granting an unimplemented keyword would be a no-op dressed as an implementation. | 2026-08-01 | **The coverage measure was lying about `[Deflect]`, and the fix was to stop it lying rather than to implement the keyword.** Went in to build the target-dependent rainbow-Power cost dimension; measured first, and the measurement said not to. `[Deflect]` unblocks **zero** cards — all five inert cards carrying it are inert for unrelated text of their own (Volibear's attack trigger, Spirit's Refuge's buff, Qiyana's conquer trigger, Deadbloom's placement rule, Ledros's kill-N additional cost). Its real cost was over-reporting: `implementableText` strips bracketed keywords because a keyword is implemented in the rules engine rather than an effect registry — an assumption never checked. Asked, per keyword, which modules consult it: **twelve of thirteen have a real consumer; `[Deflect]`'s only two mentions are the table that grants it and a doc comment recording its absence.** So Pouty Poro, whose entire text is `[Deflect]`, reported implemented while doing nothing — in a precon deck, ×2 — and Fiora - Victorious reported finished on the strength of the `[Ganking]`/`[Shield]` two thirds that work. Added `UNIMPLEMENTED_KEYWORDS` to coverage.ts: an unimplemented keyword survives the strip, so it reads as text still to write, and `isCardImplemented` derives partial-ness from it rather than needing six hand-written `PARTIALLY_IMPLEMENTED` entries as those five cards land. In-scope inert **98 → 100** (both lies corrected); tests **1082 → 1088**. Deleting the one map entry flips all seven cards when the keyword lands. | 2026-07-31 | **Tuned the AI by win rate rather than by argument, and three of the four things I was confident about were wrong.** Built `scratchpad/ai-ab.mjs` first — candidate vs baseline over all 49 deck pairings in both seats, 3000 games a candidate — because the standing rule here is no speculative heuristic without a real evaluative basis, and "a card in hand feels like about 2 Might" is exactly what that rule exists to keep out. Results: a SMALL `cardInHand` weight wins (52.2% ± 1.8 at 0.25/0.5/0.75, a flat plateau, so it works as a tie-breaker like the existing sub-1 DAMAGE_WEIGHT); a large one LOSES badly (46.3% at 2, 34.0% at 4) because valuing cards makes the AI hoard and hoarding loses — the opposite of what my stated rationale for adding it predicted. Adopted `cardInHand: 0.5, permanentInPlay: 0.5`. Confirmed in reverse: with the new weighting as baseline, the old one loses at 47.8%, the mirror image of the same measurement. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Two-ply lost, then won, and still is not worth shipping.** Modelling the opponent's best reply instead of assuming they pass measured 46.6% ± 1.8 — worse. The cause was a mispricing rather than the search: with `cardInHand` at 0 the modelled opponent answers back for FREE, so the AI over-feared a reply that really costs them a card. Priced properly the same search wins (52.0%), a swing of over five points — but that is indistinguishable from `cardInHand` alone (52.2%) while costing ~5x the compute per action. Kept, off by default, with the finding recorded: worth revisiting when the pool has enough Reaction cards that a reply is common rather than occasional. This replaces the standing note in `heuristic-ai.ts` that the opponent-passes assumption was an unmeasured known consequence. |
| 2026-07-31 | **The AI ignoring gear turned out to be almost correct.** It played ZERO gear in 60 games because `evaluate` scored only points and Might, so a gear tied with Pass and ties go to Pass. Two fixes were tried. A flat per-permanent weight makes it play gear but is a fudge — it says every gear is worth the same. Real recognition (`abilityValue`) applies the gear's activated ability through the real executor and prices the gear at what using it would actually achieve; with it on, the AI plays Orb of Regret — the one gear in the pool with an activated ability — and correctly declines the four whose value waits on a future trigger (Mushroom Pouch, Mistfall, Mask of Foresight, Scrapheap), which a position evaluator has nothing to price. But EVERY configuration that plays more gear lands at ~50% against 52.2% for the ones that do not. The recognition is accurate; the gear is marginal. Left off by default, kept as the right mechanism for when the pool has gear worth playing — and because turning it on is what makes gear reachable in self-play probes, which today it is not. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Every card in every preset deck now does what it says — in-scope inert reached zero.** It took four cards (Charm, Mask of Foresight, Udyr - Wildman, Heimerdinger - Inventor), two new primitives (**Stun**, and Legend activated abilities) and a fix to the measure that had been hiding the rest. Notes worth keeping: Udyr's four modes target differently from one another, so targeting moved onto the MODE and every ability is now normalised into a list of modes — one code path instead of a modal branch in each of enumeration, validation and execution. Charm's move is not a Standard Move (414.3.a/316.7.b), so it does not exhaust and contests for the moved unit's controller (450). Mask of Foresight's "attacks or defends alone" had to be an EVENT rather than the continuous modifier Wielder of Water's near-identical text uses, because "+1 this turn" survives a reinforcement arriving later. Heimerdinger exhausts HIMSELF (414.5), which is also why he can use an ability off an already-spent gear. A reachability gap came with it and was closed the same way Gear's was: `legalActions` offered the Legend ability, the AI used it, and the human had no way to click it — the legend renders in the side column, which had no handler at all. In-scope inert **7 → 0**; tests **779 → 823**. |
| 2026-07-31 | **The coverage measure was blind to Legends, and had been reporting three inert preset legends as implemented.** `CardDefinitionBase` declared `text` on Unit, Spell and Gear separately and omitted it from Legend, so `implementableText` saw an empty string for every Legend in the pool and `needsImplementation` answered no. Jinx - Loose Cannon, Lee Sin - Blind Monk and Viktor - Herald of the Arcane — all three OGN legends added with their starter decks — did nothing at all. Same trust-anchor failure fixed earlier for card effects, in the opposite and worse direction: that one greyed out working cards, this one reported dead ones as fine. `text` now lives on the base where a new card type cannot forget it, and a coverage-drift case asserts every type carries one. In-scope inert went **4 → 7** on the fix, which is the measure working. |
| 2026-07-31 | Three bugs found by the tests written alongside the work, all invisible in play. **`exhaustActivated` never touched the legend zone**, so a Legend ability paid no cost and was repeatable within a turn — the effect still happened, which is the worst shape for this. **Udyr - Wildman had [Ganking] printed permanently**: the loader sees brackets, and "[Ganking]" sits inside one of four MODES he must spend a buff to choose, so he could move battlefield-to-battlefield all game for free. That is the fourth card of that exact family (after Raging Soul, Bilgewater Bully and Fiora - Victorious) and the deepest — nested inside a mode list rather than merely inside a condition. And **the Spell-destination validator assumed any destination meant token placement**, so Charm was refused by a "battlefields you control" rule that has nothing to do with moving somebody else's unit. |
| 2026-07-31 | **A reachability measurement was wrong, and the correction generalises.** Probes that wrap an engine internal (a trigger's `resolve`) also count the heuristic AI's LOOKAHEAD, which applies every candidate action through the real executors in order to score it. Scrapheap's "played 259 times" was the AI considering it. Measured from the real state stream the answer is 0 — because **the AI never plays gear at all**: 60 self-play games, 828 Units and 386 Spells played, zero Gear. A 1-ply board evaluator cannot price a permanent whose value is conditional and later, so Orb of Regret, Mushroom Pouch, Scrapheap, Mask of Foresight and Mistfall are all unreached by the AI while working correctly for a human. Recorded as an AI limitation, not an engine one — and reachability probes should count from the action or state stream, never from inside the engine. |
| 2026-07-31 | **The engine can stop and ask.** Every choice here was previously decided BEFORE an action was submitted and fanned out by `legal-actions`, which needs an action to hang the choice on — so a "you may pay" on a TRIGGER, or a choice belonging to the OPPONENT, was unimplementable, and every trigger-driven discard took the front of your hand and said so apologetically. `engine/decisions.ts` adds one FIFO queue, one `AnswerDecision` action and one registry; the answer arrives as an ordinary action, so the human, the AI and the tests all answer through the same door. The frozen Java oracle is the argument for "one": it grew FIVE parallel pending-choice mechanisms, each with its own action type, validator branch, AI branch and line in a five-way if-chain answering "who acts now". Its resume is a Java closure, which is unusable here — states are immutable snapshots the AI clones and rescores — so the continuation is a `kind` string instead. Rule **321** decided the hard part outright: no Cleanup runs while a question is open, and **322** is why one at the end suffices. A question with a single answer is executed rather than asked, which is also what lets a queued follow-up task exist without a second mechanism. Cards: **Flame Chompers**, **Mistfall** (a new `unitBuffed` event, fired from `addBuff` and correctly silent on a 702.3.a no-op) and **Cull the Weak** (both players asked, in APNAP order, at resolution rather than fanned out at cast — the opponent can respond on the chain in between). Trigger-driven discards now ask. In-scope inert **7 → 4**; tests **735 → 775**. |
| 2026-07-31 | Found while building on it: **playing a card from Hidden burned floating Energy**. Rule 811 says the base cost is IGNORED, and `validate-play-card` prices it at `{0, 0}` accordingly — but the executor went on deducting floating resources against the PRINTED cost, so Consult the Past (4 Energy) played from Hidden with 3 floating Energy banked silently ate all three for a card that was supposed to be free. Confirmed with a probe before being called a bug. Both halves now share one `ignoresBaseCost`. Also extracted `engine/deploy.ts` — "does this unit enter ready?" (143.4.a plus its three overrides) had to be asked by Flame Chompers too, and a second copy is how a unit ends up entering ready in one place and exhausted in another for the same reason. |
| 2026-07-31 | **Scrapheap** ("When this is played, discarded, or killed, draw 1") — one card that closed three gaps. Its discard branch fires while the card is in HAND, so no walk over permanents in play could ever reach it: self-triggers are therefore keyed by the acting card's own defId, the shape `[Deathknell]` already used, rather than by the listener walk `eventTriggers` uses. It also forced gear to have a death funnel at all — `triggers.killGear` — since until now the only way a gear could leave play was the `[Temporary]` sweep, which put it silently in the trash. Reachability checked rather than assumed — and the first measurement was **wrong**, corrected on 2026-07-31: the probe wrapped the trigger's own `resolve`, which the heuristic AI calls on every candidate it scores, so it counted the AI THINKING about playing Scrapheap rather than playing it. Measured from the real state stream instead, 40 self-play games reach the DISCARDED branch 42 times and the played branch **never** — the AI plays no gear at all (see the 2026-07-31 entry). A live driver does play it in the UI and watch the hand size hold, so the branch works; it is the AI that never gets there. A coverage test that had pinned Scrapheap as its example of an unimplemented card now picks whichever card currently fits, since it had failed for the one reason a coverage test never should: coverage improving. In-scope inert **8 → 7**; tests **725 → 735**. |
| 2026-07-30 | Two more preset cards, each needing a primitive rather than an entry. **Mushroom Pouch** wanted a Beginning-Phase event — added to the bus and fired between the `[Temporary]` kill and scoring, both orderings load-bearing — and was only implementable at all because `[Hidden]` now exists to make its condition true of anything. **Fading Memories** wanted targeting across two KINDS of permanent ("a unit at a battlefield or a gear"), which became a `unitOrGear` spec on its own action field, plus `keywords` on GearInstance so a gear can carry a granted keyword, plus a gear sweep in the Beginning-Phase kill — 816 says "kill this PERMANENT", not "this unit". Adding the second event kind also made the compiler demand that every listener narrow the union, which is the type system doing the documenting. In-scope inert **10 → 8**; tests **713 → 725**. |
| 2026-07-30 | The `hidden.mjs` driver was a coin flip and is now deterministic, via the same fix that settled `timing/noncombat`: assert the rule in BOTH branches instead of retrying until the happy path comes up. A facedown card is a hostage to control (811 + Cleanup step 5) and the opponent gets a turn in between, so a run where the ground is lost is not a failed run — it is the other half of the rule, and the driver now asserts the card reached its OWNER's trash. It also reinforces the hidden battlefield first, which is what a player would do. Checked the engine before touching the driver, as the last such episode taught: 42 Hide offers and 0 invalid actions across 20 self-play games said the engine was fine. |
| 2026-07-30 | Added a caster-CHOSEN discard carried on the action, unlocking **Get Excited!** and **Brazen Buccaneer** — one field serving two roles, because both are "which card from hand": for Get Excited! the discard is the EFFECT (damage equals the discarded card's Energy cost, so the same spell does 2 or 8) and is mandatory; for Brazen Buccaneer it is an optional COST buying a 2-Energy discount. Threaded through all three hops — action, ChainEntry, ResolveEvent — plus the unit-trigger event, since a field dropped on one hop is this codebase's most repeated bug. In-scope inert **12 → 10**; tests **699 → 713**. |
| 2026-07-30 | Two enumeration bugs in that work, both found by verification rather than review, and both the same family — enumeration and validation disagreeing. First: the plain payment was computed before the discounted one and `continue`d when unaffordable, so Brazen Buccaneer at 4 runes was never offered AT ALL — the discount is what makes the card playable and the code bailed before reaching it. Caught by a test written for exactly that case. Second, and worse: the discounted cost subtracted the discount from the RAW price and stopped, skipping the floating-Energy reduction the plain path applies, so enumeration offered a 4-rune payment for a card validation priced at 3 and the AI threw mid-game. The engine suite did NOT catch that one — no test had floating Energy banked — a self-play probe did. Both now pinned. |
| 2026-07-30 | Implemented **additional costs paid as you play** — rule 805's `[Accelerate]` (Jinx - Demolitionist, Lee Sin - Centered) and Cruel Patron's mandatory "kill a friendly unit" — plus both Accelerate cards' other printed text (Jinx's discard 2, Lee Sin's buffed-neighbour aura). The distinction that shapes it is optional vs mandatory: 805 calls Accelerate an "Optional Additional Cost" so the decline variant is always emitted, while Cruel Patron has no "you may" and is therefore not offered at all when it cannot be paid. Accelerate's Power domain comes from the UNIT's domain, not its printed pip — reading the pip would have made Lee Sin's Accelerate rainbow, since his pip is 0 and `powerDomain` is null. In-scope inert **15 → 12**; tests **685 → 699**. |
| 2026-07-30 | Two test-fixture lessons from the same pass, both of which looked like code bugs first. A long-standing floating-Energy test used Jinx - Demolitionist purely for her COST, and broke the moment she gained her printed "discard 2" — which discarded the very card the test played next. The engine was right; a fixture that picks a card for its cost is a standing hazard once that card acquires an effect, so the replacement was chosen for having no on-play trigger at all. And an Accelerate affordability test asserted 4 runes could not cover 4 Energy + 2 Power; they can, because a Ready rune spent on Power still counts toward Energy in the same payment. |
| 2026-07-30 | **Playtest bug, fixed:** Sai Scout was played to a battlefield the player already controlled. "You may play me to an open battlefield" was implemented as a per-card boolean (`canPlayToOpenBattlefield`) that granted placement at ANY battlefield and never asked whether that battlefield was open — rule 170.11.c defines the word exactly: "unoccupied and uncontrolled". The reported symptom was the mild half. The serious half is that Sai Scout and Sneaky Deckhand could also be played onto a battlefield the OPPONENT held, which applies Contested and opens a Showdown — turning "play me to an open battlefield" into a free 5-Might attack the card never offers. Replaced with `mayPlaceOnOpenBattlefield`, one predicate shared by the validator and the enumerator so the two cannot drift. Eleven tests, including the reported case and the reinforce paths the fix must NOT have taken away; the negative ones assert the rejection MESSAGE, because Sai Scout also carries [Vision] and would otherwise have been refused for the wrong reason. |
| 2026-07-30 | Found by writing the test, not by reading the code: **three cards had their conditional keywords permanently on.** `parseKeywords` sees brackets, not conditions, so "While I'm buffed, I have [Ganking]" and "If you've discarded a card this turn, I have [Assault] and [Ganking]" were parsed as printed keywords — Bilgewater Bully could move battlefield-to-battlefield with no buff, Raging Soul attacked at +1 having discarded nothing, and Fiora - Victorious carried [Deflect]/[Ganking]/[Shield] unconditionally. All three were strictly better than the card reads, and had been since the loader was written. Fixed with a named `CONDITIONAL_KEYWORD_DEF_IDS` set (the same choice, for the same reason, as the existing HIDDEN_KEYWORD_FALSE_POSITIVES) plus `engine/granted-keywords.ts`, which grants them at runtime under the real condition. The grant layer is consulted by all three places that ask about keywords — combat Might, the move validator, and the move enumeration — because those were three separate reads of `unit.keywords` and each would otherwise have grown its own copy of the condition. Also implemented **Magma Wurm**, whose "other friendly units enter ready" is a continuous property of the board rather than a this-turn flag. In-scope inert **18 → 15**; tests **662 → 674**. |
| 2026-07-30 | Implemented **`[Hidden]`** (rule 811), the last large rules subsystem blocking the presets, and all five cards that needed it — Consult the Past, Fight or Flight, Hidden Blade, Stand United and Sprite Call. | 2026-07-30 | **Privacy, to the user's stated requirement:** both players may see THAT a facedown card is there; neither may see WHICH. `maskHiddenCards` replaces the opponent's hidden card with an opaque placeholder while keeping presence, owner and timing, and is applied at the AI's entry point as well as the board's — without it the AI reads the same GameState the engine holds and can play around a card it cannot legally know, which would look like the AI being sharp rather than the bug it is. The limitation is recorded rather than hidden: the engine still holds the truth behind the mask. |
| 2026-07-30 | Three bugs found by verification rather than by reading, and worth the pattern. (1) `executePlayCardInner` removed the card from the hidden zone BEFORE validating, destroying the very precondition the validator checks — every from-hidden play threw. (2) A from-hidden Sprite Call was still offered a BASE destination, which 811 forbids ("you must choose to play that unit at that battlefield"); the UI then stalled waiting for a placement decision the rules never grant. Found by the live driver, not the suite. (3) `isCardInteractable` counted only PlayCard candidates, so a `[Hidden]` card you cannot AFFORD — precisely the card worth hiding — could not be armed and its Hide button could never appear. | 2026-07-30 | Built the **event bus** the next fan-out needs and cleared three more preset cards. `triggers.ts` gains a `GameEvent` union and `dispatchEvent`, with listeners living in a per-domain `eventTriggers` export — so adding an event listener never means editing a shared file, which is the only reason the one-file-one-owner rule survives contact with events. `executePlayCard` fires `cardPlayed` from a **wrapper** rather than at each of its three `return`s, because this codebase has already shipped a bug of exactly that shape (a field forwarded on two hops and dropped on the third). Cards: **Vi - Destructive** (the first activation cost that is NOT an exhaust — a Recycle, rule 416/416.3, so she is repeatable while her trash lasts; assuming the exhaust would have capped her at once per turn), **Viktor - Innovator** (only reachable at all because of reaction-speed timing — a plain card cannot legally be played on someone else's turn, which the first version of its test discovered by being correctly rejected), and **Wraith of Echoes** (a per-turn flag on PlayerState, the shape the Java oracle carries under nearly the same name). In-scope inert **26 → 23**; tests **608 → 622**. |

| 2026-07-30 | Fixed a "you may" that had quietly become "you must". Wildclaw Shaman's optional buff-spend had to ride on its ordinary target field, because `cardHasOptionalExhaustCost` was gated on `card.kind === "Spell"` at BOTH call sites — so a Unit trigger could not express an optional cost at all. Declining meant naming a unit whose buff couldn't be spent, which stops existing once every friendly unit is buffed. The registry now records what each optional cost ASKS FOR (`exhaustReadyFriendly` vs `spendBuffFriendly` — a Ready unit and a Buffed unit are different sets), both gates are gone, `UnitTriggerEvent` carries `additionalCostUnitInstanceId`, and `legal-actions` always emits the decline variant. Rule **355.10.c** is the citation: a cost is not a target, so the Shaman's targeting is now `"none"`. Found by the agent that hit it and reported it rather than working around it — which is what the "STOP and report" instruction in the brief was for. |
| 2026-07-30 | Closed the activated-ability **reachability** gap this pass had opened and named: coverage said Orb of Regret was implemented, the engine and the AI both used it, and a human could not. | 2026-07-30 | Split **Buff** from **"+X Might this turn"** (rules 701-705). They were one field, `.bonus`, which `runEnd` wiped every turn — correct for a this-turn modifier and wrong for a Buff, and it made "While I'm buffed" / "spend a buff" / "Other buffed friendly units" unrepresentable. `buffed` is a boolean, not a count, because rule 702.3 says "There can only be one Buff on a Unit at a time" and 702.3.a makes a second one a no-op, which is exactly what every buffing card's reminder text describes. Renamed `.bonus` → `.mightThisTurn` so the two can't be confused again (~20 call sites, every fixture, the AI's Might sums and the board's status badges). Proved end-to-end with the first card to write a Buff (Pit Rookie) and the first to read one (Wizened Elder, whose "an ADDITIONAL +1" is +2 over printed Might, not +1). |

No test caught it because no domain file had registered a Unit before. This is precisely the failure a parallel per-card pass would have walked into and blamed on their own card. Pinned by a test that asserts the trigger FIRES, not merely that the registry lists it. |
| 2026-07-30 | Collapsed the three independent death sites (direct damage, kill instruction, combat) into `effect-helpers.killUnit` and implemented **[Deathknell]** (rule 808) on top of it. The funnel also strips the Buff before trashing (705) and captures the dying unit's location before it moves (808.1.d.3). Rule 808.1.d.1 — a replaced death fires nothing — falls out for free, since a warded unit returns before the trigger step. New `engine/triggers.ts` adds the shared listener walk, which unlike the four older dispatch tables **includes `activeGear`**; a gear-only event previously had nowhere to be heard. |
| 2026-07-30 | Implemented **[Temporary]** (rule 816) as a Beginning-Phase step ordered **before** `scoreHolds`. The ordering is the entire keyword: after scoring, every Temporary token banks a free point on the way out, inverting what the cards are for. Only the active player's die (816's "this permanent's controller's"), which is what makes granting an enemy unit Temporary delayed rather than instant removal. Routed through `destroyUnit` so a Temporary unit with a Deathknell still fires it. |

Also corrected the AI: it filtered every `ActivateAbility` out of its candidate pool on "a 1-ply board evaluator can't price a banked resource", which was right for Lux - Crownguard's Energy and wrong the moment a gear ability moved Might — that IS board state. `banksResource` is now the line, and the AI plays Orb of Regret. **Named as a divergence:** no UI affordance exists, so a human still can't activate anything. |
| 2026-07-30 | Two live Playwright drivers were failing on **stale assumptions, not regressions**, and both were fixed to assert the rule rather than the happy path. `timing/noncombat` asserted control is always established when a Non-Combat Showdown closes; rule 348.2.a is conditional on "only one player's Units remain", and the AI now reliably answers a walk-in with removal during the window — zero units means no control, correctly. `chain/human` asserted the enemy unit dies to Firestorm's 3 damage (only true at ≤3 Might) and then, after a fix, that damage is still visible afterwards (by which point `runEnd` has healed the board). The damage math is owned deterministically by the engine suite; the driver's job is the chain viewer. Both now pass repeatedly — noncombat 3/3 across both branches of 348.2.a, chain/human 6/6. |

So reaction-speed is blocked by *permission checks*, not by chain machinery. Pinned the timing model (806/813) and corrected the affected-spell count from 7 to **18 of 21**. Filed three implementation traps. |
| 2026-07-29 | Built the chain viewer (`ChainView` + the engine's `describeChain`), so rule 340.1's LIFO order is now *shown* rather than inferred — `state.spellChain` previously reached the UI nowhere at all. No rules behaviour changed. |
| 2026-07-29 | Randomized turn order per game (rule 115 — it was hardcoded to player 0, so the human always went first) and added the Best-of-1 / Best-of-3 lobby option, which is really the choice between the two sanctioned 1v1 modes: Duel (485) and Match (486). Randomizing required a real `firstPlayerIndex` on GameState first: BOTH the going-second Channel bonus and the turn counter tested against literal indices, so without it the compensation rune would have gone to the player going *first* and `turnNumber` would have miscounted. Implemented 486.5's per-game battlefield selection and 486.5's elimination across a Match. Six new tests fail against the old literal-index code. |
| 2026-07-30 | Taught the AI's evaluator to see marked damage (`DAMAGE_WEIGHT`), from a playtest report that it aimed Singularity at its own unit. Root cause: `effectiveMight` ignores `damage`, so only LETHAL damage scored — every non-lethal hit was exactly 0, making all four target choices a tie, and ties fall to enumeration order, which lists base units before battlefield ones. Weighted **below** a point of Might on purpose, so damage breaks ties but can never outbid a kill (chip 6 onto an 8-Might unit = +1.5; killing a 4-Might unit = +4) — full weight would prefer chip damage that heals at end of turn over permanent removal. Third instance of one family: `Pass` winning ties, attacks scoring 0, now targets tying. Spell casts 264 → 313, termination still 40/40. |
| 2026-07-30 | Fixed 3 of the 4 precon card gaps: Lecturing Yordle's "draw 1" and Stormclaw Ursine's "channel 1 rune exhausted" (both silently doing nothing — new `channelRunesExhausted` helper, since the Channel *Phase* always reveals Ready), and `[Tank]`'s damage-assignment order. `[Deflect]` deliberately left open — see its Divergent row; it needs a target-dependent rainbow-Power cost dimension, which is a bigger change than the other three combined. Also **corrected this file**: the damage-assignment row claimed "even distribution, no ordering", but `combat.distribute` already implemented 465.2.c's lethal-first-and-capped model correctly — only the Tank ordering was missing. 5 of the 8 new tests fail without the fixes. |
| 2026-07-30 | Audited card-effect coverage: of 288 playable OGN+OGS cards, 255 carry real rules text (33 are keyword-only), **40 implemented and 215 silently inert** — of which **210 are outside the precons**. Note a silently-inert card is indistinguishable from a working one in play — the honesty problem this file exists for, one level down. |
| 2026-07-29 | Implemented Non-Combat Showdowns and `[Action]`/`[Reaction]` timing — one epic, because they're one mechanism: rule 316.8.c makes a Showdown "a structured Window of Opportunity where Players may play cards with Action or Reaction", so the empty-battlefield Showdown IS the venue Action speed needs. Contested became real state (450/190.3.a) applied by the move, play-unit and token paths; the Cleanup stages the Showdown (323.8/344.1) and decides its kind; closing dispatches to Combat (348.1) or to 348.2.a's control establishment. | 2026-07-29 | Implemented the Cleanup's control-lapse step (`engine/cleanup.ts`, rule 323.6 step 4 / 190.4.c) after hitting the resulting freeze live. Control was previously only ever lost through combat's mutual-wipe branch, so moving or recalling your last unit away left a battlefield controlled-but-unoccupied — a state the rules don't allow, and a scoring dead end (its controller can't Hold it, since `isHeldBy` needs units present, and can't Conquer it, since they already control it). Two battlefields in that state plus empty rune decks froze the game. Fixed: **passive games 6/16 stalled → 0/16; self-play 38/40 → 40/40 terminating**, 0 invalid actions. Confirmed beforehand that it was NOT an AI regression — reproduced identically with and without the lookahead fix, same seeds and turn numbers. Four of the ten new tests fail without the lapse. Also corrected the Burn Out row, which this had been wrongly blamed on. |
| 2026-07-29 | Fixed the AI's blindness to deferred resolution, found while verifying the chain viewer (the AI-cast case needed a throwaway patch to demo at all). Both of this engine's payoffs land behind PassFocus, so scoring the state produced by applying ONE action rated a winning attack at a median of **0** and a Spell at **exactly 0** — and ties fall to `Pass`, which `legal-actions` pushes first. `chooseAction` now settles each candidate's chain/Showdown before `evaluate`. Measured over the same self-play seeds: Spell casts **0 → 87** per 40 games (13 distinct cards); Showdowns opened **3 → 41** per 12 games; winning attacks taken **0 → 25** of 41 offered. It cuts both ways by design — a losing attack settles as low as −7, so the AI now declines bad fights. Three engine tests added that each fail without the change. |
